\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\mathbb{E}\big(Z_{X}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}Z_{X}-\tr(\Sigma_{X})\big)^{2^{\ell-1}}\Big)^{2}+K(\ell,c_{*})\mathbb{E}\lVert\Sigma_{X}Z_{X}\rVert^{2^{\ell}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\mathbb{E}\big(Z_{X}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}Z_{X}-\tr(\Sigma_{X})\big)^{2^{\ell-1}}\Big)^{2}+K^{\prime}(\ell,c_{*},M)\mathbb{E}\big(Z_{X}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}Z_{X}\big)^{2^{\ell-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\mathbb{E}\big(Z_{X}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}Z_{X}-\tr(\Sigma_{X})\big)^{2^{\ell-1}}\Big)^{2}+K^{\prime}(\ell,c_{*},M)\Big[\mathbb{E}\big(Z_{X}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}Z_{X}-\tr(\Sigma)\big)^{2^{\ell-1}}+\tr(\Sigma_{X})^{2^{\ell-1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim K^{\prime}(\ell,c_{*},M)\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2^{\ell}},

where the last inequality follows from the induction hypotheses. This concludes that
\mathbb{E}L_{X}^{2^{\ell}}\lesssim_{\ell,M,c_{*}}\tau_{X}^{-2^{\ell+1}}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2^{\ell}}
.

(2). As
\lvert h(u)\rvert\lesssim u^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}R_{X}^{s}(X_{1},X_{2})=\mathbb{E}h^{s}\big(L(X_{1},X_{2})\big)\lesssim\mathbb{E}L_{X}^{2s}(X_{1},X_{2})\lesssim_{s}\tau_{X}^{-4s}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2s}.

The bound for
R_{Y}
is similar.

(3). As
\lvert h^{\prime}(u)\rvert\lesssim\lvert u\rvert/(1+u)^{1/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}(L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))^{s}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\lvert L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})\rvert^{s}}{(1+L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))^{s/2}}\bigg]

(B.1)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}^{1/2}L_{X}^{2s}(X_{1},X_{2})\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\big(1+L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})\big)^{-s}\lesssim_{M,s}\tau_{X}^{-2s}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{s}.

To calculate the last term, note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert Z_{X}\rVert^{-2s}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\lVert Z_{X}\rVert^{-2s}\bm{1}_{|\lVert Z_{X}\rVert-\mathbb{E}\lVert Z_{X}\rVert|\leq p^{1/4}}+\mathbb{E}\lVert Z_{X}\rVert^{-2s}\bm{1}_{|\lVert Z_{X}\rVert-\mathbb{E}\lVert Z_{X}\rVert|\geq p^{1/4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sqrt{p}+\mathcal{O}(p^{1/4}))^{-2s}+\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\lVert Z_{X}\rVert^{-4s}\cdot\mathbb{P}^{1/2}(|\lVert Z_{X}\rVert-\mathbb{E}\lVert Z_{X}\rVert|\geq p^{1/4})

(B.2)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{\lesssim_{s}}}p^{-s}+\varepsilon^{-4s}C(\alpha)p^{-\alpha/4}\asymp p^{-s},

by choosing
\alpha\geq 4s
. Here
(*)
follows from the exponential tail for 1-Lipschitz functionals of distributions satisfying a Poincaré inequality
[
BL09
, Corollary 4.2]
, and the following calculation: for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert Z_{X}\rVert^{-4s}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\lVert Z_{X}\rVert^{-4s}\bm{1}_{\lVert Z_{X}\rVert\geq\varepsilon}+\mathbb{E}\lVert Z_{X}\rVert^{-4s}\bm{1}_{\lVert Z_{X}\rVert<\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-4s}+\int_{\lVert x\rVert\leq\varepsilon}\lVert x\rVert^{-4s}\prod_{i=1}^{p}f(x_{i})\prod_{i=1}^{p}\mathrm{d}x_{i}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{-4s}+\big(2\pi\cdot\sup_{x\in(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)}f(x)\big)^{p}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}r^{p-1-4s}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-4s}+\big(2\pi\cdot\sup_{x\in(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)}f(x)\big)^{p}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon^{p-4s}}{p-4s}.

Hence by choosing
\varepsilon
to be a small but fixed constant (depending only on the distribution of
Z_{1}
) such that
2\pi\varepsilon\cdot\sup_{x\in(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)}f(x)<1
, we have
\mathbb{E}\lVert Z_{X}\rVert^{-4s}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-4s}
. Now using the fact that
\tau_{X}\asymp_{M}\sqrt{p}
, (
B.4
) leads to
\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}(L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))^{s}\lesssim\tau_{X}^{-s}
.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs for Section
8

C.1.
Proof of Lemma
8.1

We only prove the formula for
\psi_{X}
. Note that

\displaystyle\psi_{X}(X_{1},Y_{1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X_{2},Y_{2}}\Big[\big(R_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})-\mathbb{E}_{X}R_{X}(X_{1},X)-\mathbb{E}_{X}R_{X}(X,X_{2})+(\mathbb{E}R_{X})\big)Y_{2}^{\top}Y_{1}\Big]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ast)}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{2},Y_{2}}\Big[R_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})Y_{2}^{\top}Y_{1}\Big]=\mathbb{E}_{X_{2},Y_{2}}\Big[h(L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))Y_{2}^{\top}Y_{1}\Big].

Here in
(*)
we use the fact that
\mathbb{E}_{X_{1},X_{2},Y_{2}}R_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})(Y_{2}^{\top}Y_{1})=0
for fixed
Y_{1}
, because
R_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})(Y_{2}^{\top}Y_{1})
has equal distribution as
R_{X}(-X_{1},-X_{2})(-Y_{2}^{\top}Y_{1})
for any fixed
Y_{1}
. On the other hand, by Lemma
7.3
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{2},Y_{2}}\Big[h(L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))Y_{2}^{\top}Y_{1}\Big]=-\frac{\mathbb{E}_{X_{2},Y_{2}}\big(L_{X}^{2}(X_{1},X_{2})Y_{2}^{\top}Y_{1}\big)}{8}+\mathbb{E}_{X_{2},Y_{2}}\big[h_{3}(L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))Y_{2}^{\top}Y_{1}\big].

To compute the first term, use the representation
X_{1}=\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X},Y_{1}=\Sigma_{Y\setminus X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{Y}+\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}
in Lemma
C.1
, where
\Sigma_{Y\setminus X}\equiv\Sigma_{Y}-\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X}^{-1}\Sigma_{XY}
and
\widetilde{Z}=(\widetilde{Z}_{X}^{\top},\widetilde{Z}_{Y}^{\top})^{\top}=OZ
for some orthogonal matrix
O
described therein. Then we have

\displaystyle\tau_{X}^{4}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{X_{2},Y_{2}}\big(L_{X}^{2}(X_{1},X_{2})Y_{2}^{\top}Y_{1}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z_{X},Z_{Y}}\bigg[\Big(\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}+\lVert\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}\rVert^{2}-2X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}-\tau_{X}^{2}\Big)^{2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{E}_{Z_{X},Z_{Y}}\bigg[\Big(\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}+\lVert\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}\rVert^{2}-2X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}-\tau_{X}^{2}\Big)^{2}\widetilde{Z}_{Y}^{\top}\Sigma_{Y\backslash X}^{1/2}Y_{1}\bigg]\equiv(I)+(II).

Using symmetry of
Z
and Lemma
G.3
-(1), we have

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z_{X},Z_{Y}}\bigg[-4\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}\widetilde{Z}_{X}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad-4\lVert\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}\rVert^{2}X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}\widetilde{Z}_{X}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{1}+4\tau_{X}^{2}X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}\widetilde{Z}_{X}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle=-4\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{1}-4\Big(\tr(\Sigma_{X})\tr(X_{1}Y_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{YX})+2\tr(X_{1}Y_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X})+(m_{4}-3)\tr(H_{[11]}\circ Q_{X,1})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+4\tau_{X}^{2}X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{1}

\displaystyle=-4\Big[\big(\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}-\tr(\Sigma_{X})\big)X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{1}+2X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{1}+(m_{4}-3)\tr(H_{[11]}\circ Q_{X,1})\Big],

where

(C.1)

\displaystyle Q_{X,1}=\Sigma^{-1/2}\begin{pmatrix}\Sigma_{X}X_{1}Y_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}&\Sigma_{X}X_{1}Y_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X}^{-1}\Sigma_{XY}\\
\Sigma_{YX}X_{1}Y_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}&\Sigma_{YX}X_{1}Y_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X}^{-1}\Sigma_{XY}\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{-1/2}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z_{X},Z_{Y}}\bigg[-4\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}\widetilde{Z}_{Y}^{\top}\Sigma_{Y\backslash X}^{1/2}Y_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad-4\lVert\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}\rVert^{2}X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}\widetilde{Z}_{Y}^{\top}\Sigma_{Y\backslash X}^{1/2}Y_{1}+4\tau_{X}^{2}X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}\widetilde{Z}_{Y}^{\top}\Sigma_{Y\backslash X}^{1/2}Y_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle=-4(m_{4}-3)\tr(H_{[11]}\circ Q_{X,2}),

where

(C.2)

\displaystyle Q_{X,2}=\Sigma^{-1/2}\begin{pmatrix}0&\Sigma_{X}X_{1}Y_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{Y\backslash X}\\
0&\Sigma_{YX}X_{1}Y_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{Y\backslash X}\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{-1/2}.

The claim follows by noting that
Q_{X,1}+Q_{X,2}=Q_{X}
. The collection of identities follow from direct calculation.∎

Lemma C.1
.

Suppose
(X^{\top},Y^{\top})^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\Sigma^{1/2}Z
, where
\Sigma
is a p.s.d. matrix in
\mathbb{R}^{(p+q)\times(p+q)}
and
Z=(Z_{X}^{\top},Z_{Y}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{p+q}
has i.i.d. components with mean
0
and variance
1
. Then there exists some orthogonal matrix
O\in\mathbb{R}^{(p+q)\times(p+q)}
such that with
\widetilde{Z}\equiv(\widetilde{Z}_{X}^{\top},\widetilde{Z}_{Y}^{\top})^{\top}\equiv OZ
, we have
(X^{\top},Y^{\top})^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}((\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X})^{\top},(\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{X}+\Sigma_{Y\backslash X}^{1/2}\widetilde{Z}_{Y})^{\top})^{\top}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.

By taking
O=[\Sigma_{X}^{1/2},\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{XY};0,\Sigma_{Y\backslash X}^{1/2}]\Sigma^{-1/2}
which satisfies
OO^{\top}=O^{\top}O=I_{p+q}
, we have
(X^{\top},Y^{\top})^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\Sigma^{1/2}O^{\top}OZ
, where
\Sigma^{1/2}O^{\top}=[\Sigma_{X}^{1/2},0;\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2},\Sigma_{Y\backslash X}^{1/2}]
, as desired.
∎

C.2.
Proof of Lemma
8.3

Let
\mathcal{M}^{d}
be the set of
d\times d
matrices and
\mathcal{M}_{+}^{d}
the set of p.s.d. matrices of size
d\times d
. We need the following matrix derivative formulae.

Lemma C.2
.

Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}
.

(1)

Suppose that
S:I\to\mathcal{M}_{d}
is a smooth map. Then for any integer
m\in\mathbb{N}
, and
t\in\mathrm{int}(I)
,

(C.3)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)^{m}=\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}S(t)^{k}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)S(t)^{m-1-k}.

(2)

Suppose that
S:I\to\mathcal{M}_{d}
is a smooth map. Then for any
t\in\mathrm{int}(I)
,

(C.4)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}e^{S(t)}=\int_{0}^{1}e^{uS(t)}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)e^{(1-u)S(t)}\,\mathrm{d}u.

(3)

Suppose that
S:I\to\mathcal{M}^{d}_{+}
is a smooth map. Then for any
t\in\mathrm{int}(I)
such that
S(t)
is p.d.,

(C.5)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S^{1/2}(t)=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-uS^{1/2}(t)}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)e^{-uS^{1/2}(t)}\,\mathrm{d}u.

These formulae are well-known. We provide a self-contained proof below for the convenience of the reader.

Proof.

(1). We prove the claim by induction on
m
. The base case
m=1
is trivial, so we suppose the claim holds up to
m-1
. Now differentiating on both sides of the identity
S(t)^{m}=S(t)^{m-1}S(t)
and using the induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)^{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)^{m-1}S(t)+S(t)^{m-1}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)

\displaystyle=\bigg[\sum_{k=0}^{m-2}S(t)^{k}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)S(t)^{m-2-k}\bigg]S(t)+S(t)^{m-1}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)=\hbox{RHS of (\ref{ineq:mat_poly_int_formula})}.

(2). Using the expansion
e^{A}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}A^{k}/k!
,

RHS of (
C.4
)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{k,\ell}\frac{1}{k!\ell!}\big(uS(t)\big)^{k}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)\big((1-u)S(t)\big)^{\ell}\,\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ast)}}{{=}}\sum_{k,\ell}\frac{1}{(k+\ell+1)!}S(t)^{k}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)S(t)^{\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}S(t)^{k}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)S(t)^{m-1-k}\quad\hbox{(rearraging the summation)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m!}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)^{m}=\hbox{LHS of (\ref{ineq:mat_exp_int_formula})}.

Here in the last line we used (1), whereas in
(\ast)
we used the simple fact that
\int_{0}^{1}u^{k}(1-u)^{\ell}\,\mathrm{d}u=k!\ell!/(k+\ell+1)!
that can be proved by induction on
\ell
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}u^{k}(1-u)^{\ell}\,\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}u^{k}(1-u)^{\ell-1}(1-u)\,\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}u^{k}(1-u)^{\ell-1}\,\mathrm{d}u-\int_{0}^{1}u^{k+1}(1-u)^{\ell-1}\,\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=\frac{k!(\ell-1)!}{(k+\ell)!}-\frac{(k+1)!(\ell-1)!}{(k+\ell+1)!}=\frac{k!\ell!}{(k+\ell+1)!}.

(3). The derivative
D(t)\equiv\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S^{1/2}(t)
solves the Sylvester equation

(C.6)

\displaystyle D(t)S^{1/2}(t)+S^{1/2}(t)D(t)=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t).

We will verify
D(t)
given by the right hand side of (
C.5
) satisfies (
C.6
). As
S(t)
is p.d., the integral in (
C.5
) converges. Now we compute

\displaystyle\bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-uS^{1/2}(t)}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)e^{-uS^{1/2}(t)}\,\mathrm{d}u\bigg)S^{1/2}(t)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+S^{1/2}(t)\bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-uS^{1/2}(t)}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)e^{-uS^{1/2}(t)}\,\mathrm{d}u\bigg)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}u}\bigg[e^{-uS^{1/2}(t)}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t)e^{-uS^{1/2}(t)}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}u=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}S(t),

as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.3
: compact spectrum case.

We may represent
(X_{\ell}^{\top},Y_{\ell}^{\top})^{\top}=\Sigma^{1/2}Z_{\ell}
for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}\equiv Z_{k}-Z_{\ell}
, where
k,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, and

(C.7)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{0}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\Sigma_{X}&0\\
0&\Sigma_{Y}\end{pmatrix},\quad\Sigma_{t}\equiv\Sigma_{0}+t\begin{pmatrix}0&\Sigma_{XY}\\
\Sigma_{YX}&0\end{pmatrix}=\Sigma_{0}+t(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]}),

and
H_{[ij]}(t)\equiv\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}I_{[ij]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}
. Then

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathfrak{h}_{X},\mathfrak{h}_{Y}}(t\Sigma_{XY})\equiv\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathfrak{h}_{X}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}^{\top}H_{[11]}(t)\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\bigg)\mathfrak{h}_{Y}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}^{\top}H_{[22]}(t)\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\bigg)\bigg\lvert Z_{1}\bigg].

By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\psi_{\mathfrak{h}_{X},\mathfrak{h}_{Y}}(t\Sigma_{XY})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathfrak{h}_{X}^{\prime}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}^{\top}H_{[11]}(t)\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\bigg)\mathfrak{h}_{Y}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}^{\top}H_{[22]}(t)\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\bigg)\tau_{X}^{-2}\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}H_{[11]}(t)\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}\bigg\lvert Z_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathfrak{h}_{X}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}^{\top}H_{[11]}(t)\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\bigg)\mathfrak{h}_{Y}^{\prime}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}^{\top}H_{[22]}(t)\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\bigg)\tau_{Y}^{-2}\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}H_{[11]}(t)\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}\bigg\lvert Z_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv B_{1}(t)+B_{2}(t).

Now we compute the term
\mathrm{d}H_{[11]}(t)/\mathrm{d}t
in
B_{1}
. Using Lemma
C.2
-(3) and the fact that
(\mathrm{d}\Sigma_{t}/\mathrm{d}t)_{k,\ell}=\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}\cdot(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})\cdot e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}\mathrm{d}u.

This entails

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}H_{[11]}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\big)=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\cdot I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}+\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}I_{[11]}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}

(C.8)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{T}\bigg[e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}u.

Here for a square matrix
A
,
\mathcal{T}(A)\equiv A+A^{\top}
. This implies

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}B_{1}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\tau_{X}^{-2}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathfrak{h}_{X}^{\prime}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}^{\top}H_{[11]}(t)\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\bigg)\mathfrak{h}_{Y}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}^{\top}H_{[22]}(t)\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathcal{T}\bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty}\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}^{\top}e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}\big(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]}\big)e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}\,\mathrm{d}u\bigg)\bigg\lvert Z_{1}\bigg]\,\mathrm{d}t.

To continue calculations, we may write
e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}
as follows: by Lemma
C.2
-(2),
A\mapsto e^{A}
as a map from
\mathbb{R}^{(p+q)\times(p+q)}
to
\mathbb{R}^{(p+q)\times(p+q)}
has Frechét derivative
[\nabla(e^{\cdot})](A)\cdot H=\int_{0}^{1}e^{vA}He^{(1-v)A}\,\mathrm{d}v
, so a first-order Taylor expansion yields that

\displaystyle e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}-e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}=\int_{0}^{1}\big[\nabla(e^{\cdot})\big]\big(-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}+s(-u)(\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}-\Sigma_{0}^{1/2})\big)\cdot\big[(-u)(\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}-\Sigma_{0}^{1/2})\big]\mathrm{d}s

(C.9)

\displaystyle=(-u)\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}e^{-uv[\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}+s(\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}-\Sigma_{0}^{1/2})]}(\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}-\Sigma_{0}^{1/2})e^{-u(1-v)[\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}+s(\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}-\Sigma_{0}^{1/2})]}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}s\equiv\mathscr{R}_{1}(u,t).

On the other hand, by Lemma
C.2
-(3),
A\mapsto A^{1/2}
as a map from p.s.d. matrices in
\mathbb{R}^{(p+q)\times(p+q)}
to itself has Frechét derivative
[\nabla(\cdot)^{1/2}](A)\cdot H=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-zA^{1/2}}He^{-zA^{1/2}}\,\mathrm{d}z
, so again a first-order Taylor expansion yields that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}-\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\big[\nabla(\cdot)^{1/2}\big]\big(\Sigma_{0}+w(\Sigma_{t}-\Sigma_{0})\big)\cdot(\Sigma_{t}-\Sigma_{0})\,\mathrm{d}w

(C.10)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-z(\Sigma_{0}+w(\Sigma_{t}-\Sigma_{0}))}(\Sigma_{t}-\Sigma_{0})e^{-z(\Sigma_{0}+w(\Sigma_{t}-\Sigma_{0}))}\,\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}w\equiv\mathscr{R}_{2}(t).

Now using (
C.2
)-(
C.2
) and that

\displaystyle e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}\big(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]}\big)e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
e^{-u\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}}\Sigma_{YX}e^{-u\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}&0\end{pmatrix},

we arrive at

\displaystyle M(u,t)\equiv e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}\big(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]}\big)e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
e^{-u\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}}\Sigma_{YX}e^{-u\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}}\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}&0\end{pmatrix}+e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}\big(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]}\big)e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\mathscr{R}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{R}_{1}(u,t)\big(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]}\big)e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}+e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}\big(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]}\big)\mathscr{R}_{1}(u,t)I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}.

To control the remainder terms, we have

(C.11)

\displaystyle\lVert\mathscr{R}_{1}(u,t)\rVert_{F}\vee\lVert\mathscr{R}_{2}(t)\rVert_{F}\lesssim_{M}t(u\vee 1)\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F},

where we used the following: as
\inf_{t\in[0,1]}\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma_{t})\geq\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma)\wedge\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma_{0})\gtrsim_{M}1
,

•

\sup_{w\in[0,1]}\int_{0}^{\infty}\lVert e^{-z(\Sigma_{0}+w(\Sigma_{t}-\Sigma_{0}))}\rVert_{\op}^{2}\mathrm{d}z\lesssim_{M}1
.

•

\sup_{s,v\in[0,1]}\sup_{u\geq 0}\lVert e^{-uv[\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}+s(\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}-\Sigma_{0}^{1/2})]}\rVert_{\op}\vee\lVert e^{-u(1-v)[\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}+s(\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}-\Sigma_{0}^{1/2})]}\rVert_{\op}\lesssim_{M}1
.

Hence by using again
\lVert e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}\rVert_{\op}\vee\lVert e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}\rVert_{\op}\leq e^{-c_{0}u}
for some
c_{0}=c_{0}(M)>0
, there exist another positive
c_{1}
only depending on
M
such that

\displaystyle\lvert\tr M(u,t)\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}e^{-c_{1}u}\lVert\Sigma_{[12]}\rVert_{F}\big(\lVert\mathscr{R}_{1}(u,t)\rVert_{F}\vee\lVert\mathscr{R}_{2}(t)\rVert_{F}\big)\lesssim_{M}t(u\vee 1)e^{-c_{1}u}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2},

\displaystyle\lVert M(u,t)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}e^{-c_{1}u}\lVert\Sigma_{[12]}\rVert_{F}\big(\lVert\mathscr{R}_{1}(u,t)\rVert_{\op}\vee\lVert\mathscr{R}_{2}(t)\rVert_{\op}\vee 1\big)\lesssim_{M}t(u\vee 1)e^{-c_{1}u}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}(\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}\vee 1),

which further leads to, with
\bar{M}(t)\equiv\int_{0}^{\infty}M(u,t)\mathrm{d}u
,

\displaystyle\big|\tr\big(\bar{M}(t)\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}|\tr(M(u,t))|\lesssim_{M}t\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2},\,\big\lVert\bar{M}(t)\big\rVert_{F}\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\lVert M(u,t)\rVert_{F}\mathrm{d}u\lesssim_{M}t\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}.

Therefore by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}B_{1}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\Big)^{2}\lesssim\tau_{X}^{-4}\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\mathfrak{h}_{X}^{\prime}(L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))^{8}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\mathfrak{h}_{Y}^{8}(L_{Y}(Y_{1},Y_{2}))\cdot\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\big(Z_{1}^{\top}\bar{M}(t)Z_{1}\big)^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{X}^{-4}\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\mathfrak{h}_{X}^{\prime}(L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))^{8}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\mathfrak{h}_{Y}^{8}(L_{Y}(Y_{1},Y_{2}))\cdot\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\big[|\tr(\bar{M}(t))|\vee\lVert\bar{M}(t)\rVert_{F}\big]^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-4}\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\mathfrak{h}_{X}^{\prime}(L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))^{8}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\mathfrak{h}_{Y}^{8}(L_{Y}(Y_{1},Y_{2}))\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\big(1\vee\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\big).

Flip the roles of
X
and
Y
to conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}B_{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\Big)^{2}\lesssim_{M}\tau_{Y}^{-4}\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\mathfrak{h}_{Y}^{\prime}(L_{Y}(Y_{1},Y_{2}))^{8}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\mathfrak{h}_{X}^{8}(L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\big(1\vee\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\big).

The claim follows by noting that

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathfrak{h}_{X},\mathfrak{h}_{Y}}(\Sigma_{XY})-\psi_{\mathfrak{h}_{X},\mathfrak{h}_{Y}}(0)=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\psi_{\mathfrak{h}_{X},\mathfrak{h}_{Y}}(t\Sigma_{XY})\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{1}\big[B_{1}(t)+B_{2}(t)\big]\,\mathrm{d}t,

as desired.∎

Proof of Lemma
8.3
:
X=Y
case.

The proof is largely similar so we only sketch the key steps. Let
X_{\ell}\equiv\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}Z_{\ell}
for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. Then with
\mathfrak{h}_{X},\mathfrak{h}_{Y}
denoted as
\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}
to avoid confusion, we have

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}}(t\Sigma_{X})=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathfrak{h}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}^{\top}(t\Sigma_{X})\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\bigg)\mathfrak{g}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}^{\top}(t\Sigma_{X})\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\bigg)\bigg\lvert Z_{1}\bigg].

So

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\psi_{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}}(t\Sigma_{X})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}^{\top}(t\Sigma_{X})\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\bigg)\mathfrak{g}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}^{\top}(t\Sigma_{X})\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\bigg)\tau_{X}^{-2}\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}\bigg\lvert Z_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathfrak{h}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}^{\top}(t\Sigma_{X})\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\bigg)\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}^{\top}(t\Sigma_{X})\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\bigg)\tau_{X}^{-2}\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}\bigg\lvert Z_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv B_{1}(t)+B_{2}(t).

Using Cauchy-Schwarz directly to conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}B_{1}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\Big)^{2}\lesssim\tau_{X}^{-4}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/4}(\mathfrak{h}^{\prime}\circ L_{X})^{8}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/4}(\mathfrak{g}\circ L_{X})^{8}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Z_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}Z_{1})^{4}.

As
\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Z_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}Z_{1})^{4}\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{4}\asymp_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{4}
, the claim follows by treating
B_{2}
in a similar way.
∎

C.3.
Proof of Proposition
8.2

We first prove a few supporting lemmas for Proposition
8.2
.

Lemma C.3
.

The following hold:

(1)

We have

\displaystyle\big\lvert\mathbb{E}\big[L_{X}^{3}(X_{1},X_{2})Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2}\big]\big\rvert\lesssim_{M,\kappa}\tau_{X}^{-4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

(2)

For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
and real number
\ell>0
, there exists some constant
C=C(k,\ell,M)>0
such that for
p\geq 8\ell+1
, uniformly in
s\in[0,1]
and
r>0
,

\displaystyle\bigg\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigg[L^{k}_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})(Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2})\cdot\int_{0}^{s}\frac{(s-t)^{r}}{\big(1+tL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})\big)^{\ell}}\,\mathrm{d}t\bigg]\bigg\rvert\leq C\cdot\tau_{X}^{-k}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

Proof.

(1).
By symmetry, we have

\displaystyle\tau_{X}^{6}\mathbb{E}\big[L_{X}^{3}(X_{1},X_{2})Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\lVert X_{1}-X_{2}\rVert^{6}(Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2})+(-3\tau_{X}^{2})\cdot\mathbb{E}\lVert X_{1}-X_{2}\rVert^{4}(Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+(3\tau_{X}^{4})\cdot\mathbb{E}\lVert X_{1}-X_{2}\rVert^{2}(Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2})

\displaystyle\equiv L_{1}-3\tau_{X}^{2}L_{2}+3\tau_{X}^{4}L_{3}.

We first calculate
L_{1}
. Using again the symmetry of
Z
and the identity

(C.12)

\displaystyle\lVert X_{1}-X_{2}\rVert^{4}

\displaystyle=\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{4}+\lVert X_{2}\rVert^{4}+4(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})^{2}+2\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}\lVert X_{2}\rVert^{2}-4\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})-4\lVert X_{2}\rVert^{2}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2}),

we have

\displaystyle L_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[(\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}+\lVert X_{2}\rVert^{2}-2X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})\cdot(Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Big(\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{4}+\lVert X_{2}\rVert^{4}+4(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})^{2}+2\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}\lVert X_{2}\rVert^{2}-4\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})-4\lVert X_{2}\rVert^{2}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=-12\mathbb{E}\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{4}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})(Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2})-12\mathbb{E}\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}\lVert X_{2}\rVert^{2}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})(Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2})-8\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})^{3}(Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2}).

Now by direct calculation, we have:

•

By first taking expectation of
X_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{4}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})(Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2})=\mathbb{E}\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{4}(X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{1})=\big(\frac{1}{4}\tau_{X}^{4}+\mathcal{O}(p)\big)\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

•

By first taking expectation of
X_{1}
, we have by Lemma
G.3
-(1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}\lVert X_{2}\rVert^{2}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})(Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2})=\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{1})(X_{1}^{\top}\lVert X_{2}\rVert^{2}X_{2}Y_{2}^{\top}X_{1})

\displaystyle=\big[\frac{1}{2}\tau_{X}^{2}+\mathcal{O}(1)\big]\cdot\mathbb{E}(X_{2}^{\top}Y_{2})\lVert X_{2}\rVert^{2}=\big(\frac{1}{4}\tau_{X}^{4}+\mathcal{O}(p)\big)\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

•

By first taking expectation of
X_{1}
, we have by Lemma
G.3
-(1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})^{3}(Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2})=\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2}X_{2}^{\top}X_{2})(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2}Y_{2}^{\top}X_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}(X_{2}^{\top}Y_{2})(X_{2}^{\top}X_{2})=\mathcal{O}(p)\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

Combining the pieces yields that

\displaystyle L_{1}=-6\big(\tau_{X}^{4}+\mathcal{O}(p)\big)\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

On the other hand, direct calculation of
L_{2}
via (
C.12
) and Lemma
C.4
, and
L_{3}
yields that

\displaystyle L_{2}=-4\big(\tau_{X}^{2}+\mathcal{O}(1)\big)\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2},\quad L_{3}=-2\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

Putting together the estimates for
L_{1}
-
L_{3}
concludes that
\tau_{X}^{6}\mathbb{E}\big[L_{X}^{3}(X_{1},X_{2})Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2}\big]=\mathcal{O}(p)\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim\tau_{X}^{2}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
, as desired.

(2). We only prove the Gaussian case, where the dependence on
\Sigma_{XY}
is directly exposed via the representation
(X,Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}Z_{X},\Sigma_{Y\backslash X}^{1/2}Z_{Y}+\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X}^{-1}Z_{X})
; the general case could be dealt by the interpolation method adopted in Lemmas
8.3
and
C.4
.

The left hand side of the desired inequality equals

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[L_{X}^{k}\big(\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}Z_{1;X},\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}Z_{2;X}\big)Z_{1;X}^{\top}DZ_{2;X}\cdot\int_{0}^{s}\frac{(s-t)^{r}}{\big(1+tL_{X}(\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}Z_{1;X},\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}Z_{2;X})\big)^{\ell}}\,\mathrm{d}t\bigg],

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\mathbb{E}^{1/4}L_{X}^{4k}(X_{1},X_{2})\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Z_{1;X}^{\top}DZ_{2;X})^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\bigg[\int_{0}^{s}\frac{(s-t)^{r}}{\big(1+tL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})\big)^{\ell}}\,\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{4}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ast)}}{{\lesssim}}_{k,\ell,M}\tau_{X}^{-2k}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{k}\cdot\lVert D\rVert_{F}\cdot 1\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-k}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

Here in
(\ast)
we used the calculation: as
1+tL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})=(1-t)+t\lVert X_{1}-X_{2}\rVert^{2}/\tau_{X}^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{s}\frac{(s-t)^{r}}{\big(1+tL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})\big)^{\ell}}\,\mathrm{d}t\bigg]^{4}\lesssim\int_{0}^{s}\mathbb{E}\big(1+tL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})\big)^{-4\ell}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{s}(1-t)^{-4\ell}\wedge\big[t^{-4\ell}\mathbb{E}(\lVert X_{1}-X_{2}\rVert^{2}/\tau_{X}^{2})^{-4\ell}\big]\mathrm{d}t\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(**)}}{{\lesssim}}_{\ell}\int_{0}^{s}\big[(1-t)^{-4\ell}\wedge t^{-4\ell}\big]\mathrm{d}t\lesssim 1,

using in
(**)
a similar calculation as in (
B.4
) which holds under
p\geq 8\ell+1
.
∎

Lemma C.4
.

Recall the definitions of
P_{X},P_{Y},Q_{X},Q_{Y}
in (
8.2
). Suppose the spectrum of
\Sigma
is contained in
[M^{-1},M]
for some
M>1
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\tr(P_{X}\circ Q_{X})\vee\mathbb{E}\tr(P_{Y}\circ Q_{Y})\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

Proof of Lemma
C.4
.

By Lemma
8.1
and symmetry, it suffices to show that
\mathbb{E}\tr(P_{X}\circ Q_{X})=\tr(H_{[11]}\circ G_{[12]})\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
. The high level proof strategy is the same as Lemma
8.3
and we present the details below. Define the map
F:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

\displaystyle F(t):t\mapsto\tr(H_{[11]}(t)\circ G_{[12]}(t))\equiv\tr(\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\circ\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\Sigma_{[12]}(t)\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}).

Here
\Sigma_{t}
is given in (
C.7
), and
\Sigma_{[12]}(t)=[0,t\Sigma_{XY};0,0]
. Hence by using
F(0)=0
, we have

\displaystyle\tr(H_{[11]}\circ G_{[12]})=F(1)=\int_{0}^{1}F^{\prime}(t)\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{1}\tr\Big(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}H_{[11]}(t)\circ G_{[12]}(t)+H_{[11]}(t)\circ\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}G_{[12]}(t)\Big)\mathrm{d}t.

For the first term, we use (
C.2
) to deduce that (recall that
\mathcal{T}(A)=A+A^{\top}
)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\tr\Big(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}H_{[11]}(t)\circ G_{[12]}(t)\Big)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\tr\Big[\mathcal{T}\Big(e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\Big)\circ G_{[12]}(t)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\tr\Big[\Big(e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\Big)\circ G_{[12]}(t)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}t.

Next, using the definitions in (
C.2
) and (
C.2
), we have

\displaystyle e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}=e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}+\mathscr{R}_{1}(u,t),\quad\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}=\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}+\mathscr{R}_{2}(t),

so we may expand the integrand as
\tr\big(M(u,t)+R(u,t)\big)
, where

\displaystyle M(u,t)

\displaystyle=\Big(e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\Big)\circ\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\Sigma_{[12]}(t)\Sigma_{0}^{1/2},

\displaystyle R(u,t)

\displaystyle=e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\circ\Big(\mathscr{R}_{2}(t)\Sigma_{[12]}(t)\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}+\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\Sigma_{[12]}(t)\mathscr{R}_{2}(t)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\mathscr{R}_{2}(t)+e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})\mathscr{R}_{1}(u,t)I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\mathscr{R}_{1}(u,t)(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\Big)\circ\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\Sigma_{[12]}(t)\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}.

By direct calculation we have
\tr\big(M(u,t)\big)=0
. Furthermore, using
\tr(A\circ B)\leq\lVert A\rVert_{F}\lVert B\rVert_{F}
and the bound of
\mathscr{R}_{1}(u,t),\mathscr{R}_{2}(t)
in (
C.11
), we have
\tr(R(u,t))\leq t(u\vee 1)e^{-cu}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
for some
c=c(M)>0
. Combining the two estimates yields that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\tr\Big(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}H_{[11]}(t)\circ G_{[12]}(t)\Big)\mathrm{d}t=2\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\tr(M(u,t)+R(u,t))\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}t\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

Next, for the second term, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}G_{[12]}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}\Sigma_{[12]}(t)\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{\infty}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\Sigma_{[12]}(t)e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}\Sigma_{[12]}(t)\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\quad+\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\Sigma_{[12]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\equiv\partial G_{[12],1}(t)+\partial G_{[12],2}(t)+\partial G_{[12],3}(t).

Using similar calculations as above, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{1}\tr(H_{[11]}(t)\circ\partial G_{[12],1}(t))\mathrm{d}t\Big|\vee\Big|\int_{0}^{1}\tr(H_{[11]}(t)\circ\partial G_{[12],2}(t))\mathrm{d}t\Big|\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

For
\partial G_{[12],3}
, we may use
\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}=\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}+\mathscr{R}_{2}(t)
to expand the integrand as
\tr(M(t)+R(t))
, where

\displaystyle M(t)

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\circ\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\Sigma_{[12]}\Sigma_{0}^{1/2},

\displaystyle R(t)

\displaystyle=\big(\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}I_{[11]}\mathscr{R}_{2}(t)+\mathscr{R}_{2}(t)I_{[11]}\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\big)\circ\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\Sigma_{[12]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\circ\big(\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}\Sigma_{[12]}\mathscr{R}_{2}(t)+\mathscr{R}_{2}(t)\Sigma_{[12]}\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\big).

Direct calculation yields that
\tr(M(t))=0
and
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|\tr(R(t))|\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
. This concludes that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\tr\Big(H_{[11]}(t)\circ\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}G_{[12]}(t)\Big)\mathrm{d}t\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2},

and hence the proof is complete.
∎

We are now ready for the proof of Proposition
8.2
. We divide the proof into four parts: first moment bounds for
\psi_{X}
and
\psi_{Y}
, first moment bound for
\psi_{X,Y}
, second moment bounds for
\psi_{X}
and
\psi_{Y}
, and second moment bound for
\psi_{X,Y}
.

Proof of Proposition
8.2
-
Part 1
: first moment bounds for
\psi_{X},\psi_{Y}
.

By Lemma
8.1
, we have
\mathbb{E}\psi_{X}(X_{1},Y_{1})=\mathbb{E}A_{1,X}(X_{1},Y_{1})+\mathbb{E}A_{2,X}(X_{1},Y_{1})
with
A_{1,X},A_{2,X}
defined therein. For
\mathbb{E}A_{1,X}(X_{1},Y_{1})
, using

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{1}]=\tr(\Sigma_{X})\tr(\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX})+2\tr(\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X})+\kappa\tr(H_{[11]}\circ\mathbb{E}Q_{X}),

we have

(C.13)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}A_{1,X}(X_{1},Y_{1})=\tau_{X}^{-4}\big[2\tr(\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X})+\kappa\tr(H_{[11]}\circ\mathbb{E}Q_{X})\big].

Here
Q_{X}
is given by (
8.2
). The claim
|\mathbb{E}A_{1,X}(X_{1},Y_{1})|\lesssim_{M,\kappa}\tau_{X}^{-4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
now follows from Lemma
C.4
. Now we handle the ‘residual’ term
\mathbb{E}A_{2,X}(X_{1},Y_{1})
: By Lemmas
7.3
and
C.3
,

\displaystyle\lvert\mathbb{E}A_{2,X}(X_{1},Y_{1})\rvert\lesssim\big\lvert\mathbb{E}\big[L_{X}^{3}(X_{1},X_{2})Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2}\big]\big\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigg[L_{X}^{4}(X_{1},X_{2})Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2}\cdot\int_{0}^{1}\frac{(1-s)^{3}}{\big(1+sL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})\big)^{7/2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg]\bigg\rvert\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

The claim follows.
∎

Proof of Proposition
8.2
-
Part 2
: first moment bounds for
\psi_{X,Y}
.

By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\psi_{X,Y}=\mathbb{E}R_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})R_{Y}(Y_{1},Y_{2})-2\mathbb{E}R_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})R(Y_{1},Y_{3})+\mathbb{E}R(X_{1},X_{2})R(Y_{3},Y_{4})\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{3}S_{i}.

Next we use the same interpolation method as in Lemma
8.3
to extract the dependence on
\Sigma_{XY}
of
S_{1}
-
S_{3}
. For
S_{1}
, define the map
F_{1}:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

\displaystyle F_{1}(t):t\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{(X_{1},Y_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}Z_{1},(X_{2},Y_{2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}Z_{2}}R_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})R_{Y}(Y_{1},Y_{2}),

where
\Sigma_{t}
is defined in (
C.7
). Define
F_{2}(t)
and
F_{3}(t)
similarly. Then
\mathbb{E}\psi_{X,Y}=\sum_{i=1}^{3}F_{i}(1)
and by definition
\sum_{i=1}^{3}F_{i}(0)=0
. Since with
\bar{Z}_{1,2}\equiv Z_{1}-Z_{2}
,
H_{[11]}(t)\equiv\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}
, and a similar definition for
H_{[22]}(t)
,

\displaystyle F_{1}(t)=\mathbb{E}h\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[11]}(t)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\Big)h\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[22]}(t)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\Big),

we have
\mathrm{d}F_{1}(t)/\mathrm{d}t=\Delta_{1,1}(t)+\Delta_{1,2}(t)
, where

\displaystyle\tau_{X}^{2}\Delta_{1,1}(t)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[11]}(t)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\Big)\cdot\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}H_{[11]}(t)\bar{Z}_{1,2}\cdot h\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[22]}(t)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\Big),

\displaystyle\tau_{Y}^{2}\Delta_{1,2}(t)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbb{E}h\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[11]}(t)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\Big)\cdot\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}H_{[22]}(t)\bar{Z}_{1,2}\cdot h^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[22]}(t)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\Big).

Now using the formula of
\mathrm{d}H_{[11]}(t)/\mathrm{d}t
in (
C.2
), we have the decomposition
\tau_{X}^{2}\Delta_{1,1}(t)=M_{1,1}+R_{1,1}(t)
, where

\displaystyle M_{1,1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[11]}(0)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\Big)\cdot\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{T}\Big(e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\Big)\mathrm{d}u\bar{Z}_{1,2}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot h\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[22]}(0)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\Big)

and
R_{1,1}(t)
consists of terms of the forms

\displaystyle R_{1,1,1}(t)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbb{E}\Big[h^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[11]}(t)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\Big)-h^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[11]}(0)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\cdot\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{T}\Big(e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\Big)\mathrm{d}u\bar{Z}_{1,2}\cdot h\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[22]}(0)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\Big),

\displaystyle R_{1,1,2}(t)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[11]}(0)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\Big)\cdot\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{T}\Big(e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\big(\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}-\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\big)\Big)\mathrm{d}u\bar{Z}_{1,2}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot h\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[22]}(0)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\Big),

\displaystyle R_{1,1,3}(t)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[11]}(0)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\Big)\cdot\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{T}\Big(e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}(\Sigma_{[12]}+\Sigma_{[21]})e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}I_{[11]}\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\Big)\mathrm{d}u\bar{Z}_{1,2}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Big[h\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[22]}(t)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\Big)-h\Big(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[22]}(0)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\Big)\Big].

Using Cauchy-Schwarz, a similar calculation as in (
B.4
), and the estimates

\displaystyle\lVert H_{[11]}(t)-H_{[11]}(0)\rVert_{F}\vee\lVert\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}-\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}\rVert_{F}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2},\quad\lVert e^{-u\Sigma_{t}^{1/2}}-e^{-u\Sigma_{0}^{1/2}}\rVert_{F}\lesssim_{M}e^{-c_{0}u}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F},

we arrive at

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|R_{1,1,1}(t)|\vee|R_{1,1,2}(t)|\vee|R_{1,1,3}(t)|\lesssim_{M}\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{\tau_{X}\tau_{Y}^{2}}.

Define
R_{i,j,k}
,
i\leq 3,j\leq 2,k\leq 3
and
M_{i,j}
,
i\leq 3,j\leq 2
similarly. Flipping the roles of
X,Y
then yields that

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{\mathrm{d}F_{1}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big|\lesssim_{M}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{j=1}^{2}M_{i,j}\Big|+\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}(\tau_{X}\wedge\tau_{Y})}.

The claim now follows by noting that
\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{j=1}^{2}M_{i,j}=0
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
8.2
-
Part 3
: second moment bounds for
\psi_{X},\psi_{Y}
.

We write
A_{1,X},A_{2,X}
as
A_{1},A_{2}
in the proof. We only need to bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\psi_{X}^{2}(X_{1},Y_{1})\lesssim\big[\mathbb{E}A_{1}^{2}(X_{1},Y_{1})+\mathbb{E}A_{2}^{2}(X_{1},Y_{1})\big].

Next we bound the above two summands separately.

To bound
\var\big(A_{1}(X_{1},Y_{1})\big)
, we write

\displaystyle A_{1}(X_{1},Y_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}A_{1}(Z)=(2\tau_{X}^{4})^{-1}\Big[\big(Z^{\top}H_{[11]}Z-\tr(H_{[11]})\big)Z^{\top}\bar{G}_{[1,2]}Z+2Z^{\top}\bar{G}_{X}Z+\kappa(P_{X}\circ Q_{X})\Big].

Here
\bar{G}_{[1,2]}=(G_{[12]}+G_{[21]})/2
and

(C.14)

\displaystyle\bar{G}_{X}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}0&\Sigma_{X}\Sigma_{XY}\\
\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X}&0\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2}.

Let
(2\tau_{X}^{4})A_{1}(X_{1},Y_{1})\equiv A_{1,1}(X_{1},Y_{1})+A_{1,2}(X_{1},Y_{1})
, with
A_{1,2}(X_{1},Y_{1})=\kappa(P_{X}\circ Q_{X})
. For
A_{1,1}(X_{1},Y_{1})
, by Poincaré inequality and Lemma
7.1
, we have

\displaystyle\var\big(A_{1,1}(X_{1},Y_{1})\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}(Z^{\top}\bar{G}_{[1,2]}Z)^{2}Z^{\top}H_{[11]}^{2}Z+\mathbb{E}\big(Z^{\top}H_{[11]}Z-\tr(H_{[11]})\big)^{2}Z^{\top}\bar{G}_{[1,2]}^{2}Z+\mathbb{E}Z^{\top}\bar{G}_{X}^{2}Z

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\tr^{2}(\bar{G}_{[1,2]})+\lVert\bar{G}_{[1,2]}\rVert_{F}^{2}\big)\lVert H_{[11]}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\lVert\bar{G}_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}\big(\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}+\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\rVert_{F}^{2}\big)+\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}.

For
A_{1,2}(X_{1},Y_{1})
, note that
Q_{X}(X_{1},Y_{1})=Q_{X}(Z)=H_{[11]}ZZ^{\top}H_{[22]}
. This implies

\displaystyle\nabla_{Z}\tr(P_{X}\circ Q_{X}(Z))=\sum_{i=1}^{p+q}(H_{[11]})_{ii}\big[H_{[11]}e_{i}(e_{i}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z)+H_{[22]}e_{i}(e_{i}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z)\big]\equiv D_{1}(Z)+D_{2}(Z).

Then by the Poincaré inequality, we have
\var(A_{1,2}(X_{1},Y_{1}))\leq c_{*}\big(\mathbb{E}\lVert D_{1}(Z)\rVert^{2}+\mathbb{E}\lVert D_{2}(Z)\rVert^{2}\big)
. By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert D_{1}(Z)\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\sum_{i,j=1}^{p+q}(H_{[11]})_{ii}(H_{[11]})_{jj}e_{i}^{\top}H_{[11]}^{2}e_{j}\cdot e_{i}^{\top}H_{[22]}ZZ^{\top}H_{[22]}e_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{p+q}(H_{[11]})_{ii}(H_{[11]})_{jj}(H_{[11]}^{2}\circ H_{[22]}^{2})_{ij}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\sum_{i,j=1}^{p+q}(H_{[11]})^{2}_{ii}(H_{[11]})^{2}_{jj}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot\Big(\sum_{i,j=1}^{p+q}(H_{[11]}^{2}\circ H_{[22]}^{2})_{ij}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\lVert H_{[11]}\rVert_{F}^{2}\cdot\lVert G_{[11]}\circ G_{[22]}\rVert_{F}.

By Lemmas
7.1
and
7.2
, we have
\mathbb{E}\lVert D_{1}(Z)\rVert^{2}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\tau_{X}\rVert^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
. Hence with a similar bound for
\mathbb{E}\lVert D_{1}(Z)\rVert^{2}
, we have
\var(A_{1,2}(X_{1},Y_{1}))\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
.
This together with (
C.13
) yields that

(C.15)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}A_{1}^{2}(X_{1},Y_{1})

\displaystyle=\var\big(A_{1}(X_{1},Y_{1})\big)+\big(\mathbb{E}A_{1}(X_{1},Y_{1})\big)^{2}\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-6}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}(\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\vee 1).

Next we handle
A_{2}
. Similar to the proof of Lemma
C.3
-(2), we only show below the proof for the Gaussian case. By definition of
h_{3}
in Lemma
7.3
and the parametrization
Y_{2}=\Sigma_{Y\backslash X}^{1/2}Z_{2;Y}+\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}Z_{2;X}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}A_{2}^{2}(X_{1},Y_{1})\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{X_{1},Y_{1}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{X_{2},Y_{2}}\bigg[L_{X}^{3}(X_{1},X_{2})Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{(1-s)^{2}}{(1+sL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))^{5/2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg]\bigg\}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}^{1/4}L_{X}^{24}(X_{1},X_{2})\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Z_{2;X}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{1})^{4}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{(1-s)^{2}}{(1+sL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))^{5/2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg)^{8}.

The three terms above can be bounded as follows:

•

Lemma
7.5
yields that
\mathbb{E}^{1/4}L_{X}^{24}(X_{1},X_{2})\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-6}
.

•

With
L\equiv\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}^{1/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\big(Z_{2;X}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{1}\big)^{4}=\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\big(Z_{X}^{\top}LZ_{Y}\big)^{4}\lesssim\lVert L\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

•

By a similar calculation as in (
B.4
), for
p
larger than an absolute constant, the third term is at most of constant order.

Combining the estimates yields that

(C.16)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}A_{2}^{2}(X_{1},Y_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-6}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

Combine (
C.15
)-(
C.16
) to conclude the estimate for
\mathbb{E}\psi_{X}^{2}(X_{1},Y_{1})
. A similar argument applies to
\mathbb{E}\psi_{Y}^{2}(X_{1},Y_{1})
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
8.2
-
Part 4
: second moment bound for
\psi_{X,Y}
.

Using the notation in the proof of Lemma
8.3
, and viewing
\psi_{X,Y}
as a function of
\Sigma_{XY}
, for any
t\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\psi_{X,Y}(t\Sigma_{XY})=\mathbb{E}_{Z_{2}}\bigg[\mathcal{D}h\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[11]}(t)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\bigg)\mathcal{D}h\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{1,2}^{\top}H_{[22]}(t)\bar{Z}_{1,2}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\bigg)\bigg],

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}g(Z_{1},Z_{2})\equiv g(Z_{1},Z_{2})-\mathbb{E}_{Z_{1}}g(Z_{1},Z_{2})-\mathbb{E}_{Z_{2}}g(Z_{1},Z_{2})+\mathbb{E}_{Z_{1},Z_{2}}g(Z_{1},Z_{2}).

Expanding the product results in 16 terms of form

\displaystyle B_{(k,\ell),(k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime})}(t\Sigma_{XY})\equiv\mathbb{E}\bigg[h\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}^{\top}H_{[11]}(t)\bar{Z}_{k,\ell}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}-1\bigg)h\bigg(\frac{\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}^{\top}H_{[22]}(t)\bar{Z}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}-1\bigg)\bigg\lvert Z_{1}\bigg],

where
k\neq\ell,k^{\prime}\neq\ell^{\prime}
. Using Lemma
8.3
and the moment estimates in Lemma
7.5
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big(\psi_{X,Y}(\Sigma_{X,Y})-\psi_{X,Y}(0)\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{k,k^{\prime},\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\big(B_{(k,\ell),(k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime})}(\Sigma_{XY})-B_{(k,\ell),(k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime})}(0)\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-4}\tau_{Y}^{-4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}(1\vee\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2})(\tau_{X}\wedge\tau_{Y})^{-2}.

The claim follows as
\psi_{X,Y}(0)=0
.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs for Section
9

D.1.
Proof of Lemma
9.2

As
\mathbb{E}_{Z_{2},Z_{3},Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})=0
, Proposition
2.1
yields that

\displaystyle k_{1}(z_{1})=\frac{1}{4!}\sum_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4)}\bigg[\mathbb{E}_{Z_{2},Z_{3},Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2\mathbb{E}_{Z_{2},Z_{3},Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{3}})\bigg].

The claim now follows by calculating

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4)}\mathbb{E}_{Z_{2},Z_{3},Z_{4}}\big(U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad=12\big(\mathbb{E}U(x_{1},X)V(y_{1},Y)+\mathbb{E}U(X,X^{\prime})V(Y,Y^{\prime})\big),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z_{2},Z_{3},Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{3}})=0,

using the double-centered property of
U,V
.∎

D.2.
Proof of Lemma
9.3

Recall the identities involving
U,V
in (
7.4
). Then we have

\displaystyle g_{1}(x_{1},y_{1})\equiv\frac{1}{2}\bigg[\mathbb{E}U(x_{1},X_{2})V(y_{1},Y_{2})-\dcov^{2}(X,Y)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\tau_{X}\tau_{Y}}\bigg[\big(x_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}y_{1}-\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\big)+\big(R_{1}(x_{1},y_{1})-\mathbb{E}R_{1}(X_{1},Y_{1})\big)\bigg],

where, with
\psi_{X},\psi_{Y},\psi_{XY}
defined in (
8.1
),

\displaystyle R_{1}(x_{1},y_{1})\equiv-\tau_{X}^{2}\psi_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})-\tau_{Y}^{2}\psi_{Y}(x_{1},y_{1})+\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}\psi_{X,Y}(x_{1},y_{1}).

By Lemma
8.1
, we may write [recall the definitions of
\bar{\psi}_{X}
and
\bar{\psi}_{Y}
in (
9.1
)]

\displaystyle\psi_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\tau_{X}^{4}}\big(\lVert x_{1}\rVert^{2}-\tr(\Sigma_{X})\big)\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\bar{\psi}_{X}(x_{1},y_{1}),

\displaystyle\psi_{Y}(x_{1},y_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\tau_{Y}^{4}}\big(\lVert y_{1}\rVert^{2}-\tr(\Sigma_{Y})\big)\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\bar{\psi}_{Y}(x_{1},y_{1}).

The claim follows.∎

D.3.
Proof of Lemma
9.4

Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bar{g}_{1}^{2}(X,Y)=\frac{1}{4\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}}\Big[\mathbb{E}(X^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y-\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2})^{2}+\mathbb{E}\mathscr{A}_{1,X}(X,Y)^{2}+\mathbb{E}\mathscr{A}_{1,Y}(X,Y)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\mathbb{E}(X^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y)\mathscr{A}_{1,X}(X,Y)+2\mathbb{E}(X^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y)\mathscr{A}_{1,Y}(X,Y)+2\mathbb{E}\mathscr{A}_{1,X}(X,Y)\mathscr{A}_{1,Y}(X,Y)\Big].

The terms can be calculated as follows:

•

By Lemma
G.3
-(1), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(X^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y-\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2})^{2}=\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\tr(\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X})+\kappa\tr(G_{[12]}\circ G_{[12]}).

•

By Lemma
G.3
-(1), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathscr{A}_{1,X}(X,Y)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}}{4\tau_{X}^{4}}\cdot\big[2\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\kappa\tr(H_{[11]}\circ H_{[11]})\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{2\tau_{X}^{4}}+\frac{\kappa}{4\tau_{X}^{4}}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}\tr(H_{[11]}\circ H_{[11]}),

and similarly
\mathbb{E}\mathscr{A}_{1,Y}(X,Y)^{2}=\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{2}/(2\tau_{Y}^{4})+(4\tau_{Y}^{4})^{-1}\kappa\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}\tr(H_{[22]}\circ H_{[22]})
.

•

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(X^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y)\mathscr{A}_{1,X}(X,Y)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{2\tau_{X}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[(X^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y)(\lVert X\rVert^{2}-\tr(\Sigma_{X}))\Big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}\tr(\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X})-\frac{\kappa\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{2\tau_{X}^{2}}\tr(H_{[11]}\circ G_{[12]}),

and similarly

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(X^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y)\mathscr{A}_{1,Y}(X,Y)=-\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}\tr(\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y})-\frac{\kappa\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{2\tau_{Y}^{2}}\tr(H_{[22]}\circ G_{[12]}).

•

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathscr{A}_{1,X}(X,Y)\mathscr{A}_{1,Y}(X,Y)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}}{4\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[(\lVert X\rVert^{2}-\tr(\Sigma_{X}))(\lVert Y\rVert^{2}-\tr(\Sigma_{Y}))\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}}{4\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}}\cdot\big[2\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\kappa\tr(H_{[11]}\circ H_{[22]})\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{6}}{2\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}}+\frac{\kappa\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}}{4\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}}\tr(H_{[11]}\circ H_{[22]}).

The proof is complete.∎

D.4.
Proof of Lemma
9.5

(1). With
(X^{\top},Y^{\top})^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\Sigma^{1/2}Z
, we may write

\displaystyle\bar{g}_{1}(X,Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\bar{g}_{1}(Z)\equiv\frac{1}{4\tau_{X}\tau_{Y}}\cdot\Big[Z^{\top}AZ-\mathbb{E}(Z^{\top}AZ)\Big],

where the symmetric matrix
A
is defined by

\displaystyle A\equiv G_{[12]}+G_{[21]}-\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{\tau_{X}^{2}}H_{[11]}-\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}H_{[22]}.

So by Lemma
G.3
-(1), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bar{g}_{1}^{2}(X,Y)=\frac{1}{16\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}}\cdot\big(2\lVert A\rVert_{F}^{2}+\kappa\tr(A\circ A)\big).

Since
Z
has a Lebesgue density, we have
\kappa=\mathbb{E}Z_{1}^{4}-3=\var(Z_{1}^{2})-2>c_{0}-2
for some
c_{0}>0
that only depends on the distribution of
Z_{1}
. Hence using
\tr(A\circ A)\leq\lVert A\rVert_{F}^{2}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\bar{g}_{1}^{2}(X,Y)\geq c_{0}\lVert A\rVert_{F}^{2}/(16\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2})
, which can be bounded by

\displaystyle\lVert A\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg\lVert\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}-\big(\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}/\tau_{X}^{2}\big)I_{p}&\Sigma_{XY}\\
\Sigma_{YX}&-\big(\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}/\tau_{Y}^{2}\big)I_{q}\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2}\bigg\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{M}\bigg\lVert\begin{pmatrix}-\big(\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}/\tau_{X}^{2}\big)I_{p}&\Sigma_{XY}\\
\Sigma_{YX}&-\big(\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}/\tau_{Y}^{2}\big)I_{q}\end{pmatrix}\bigg\rVert_{F}^{2}\gtrsim\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

This concludes the lower bound claim. If the spectrum of
\Sigma
is bounded above as well, then using Lemma
G.5
, we have

•

\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
;

•

\tr(\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X})\asymp_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
;

•

\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{2\tau_{X}^{4}}\vee\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{2\tau_{Y}^{4}}\lesssim_{M}(\tau_{X}^{-2}\vee\tau_{Y}^{-2})\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}(\tau_{X}^{2}\wedge\tau_{Y}^{2})\lesssim\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
;

•

\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\tr(\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X})}{\tau_{X}^{2}}\vee\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\tr(\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y})}{\tau_{Y}^{2}}\lesssim_{M}\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}}{(\tau_{X}\wedge\tau_{Y})^{2}}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
;

•

\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{6}}{\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\frac{\tau_{X}^{4}\wedge\tau_{Y}^{4}}{\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}}\lesssim\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
;

•

\tr(G_{[12]}\circ G_{[12]})\leq\lVert G_{[12]}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
;

•

\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}}{4\tau_{X}^{4}}\tr(H_{[11]}\circ H_{[11]})\vee\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}}{4\tau_{Y}^{4}}\tr(H_{[22]}\circ H_{[22]})\lesssim_{M}\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}}{\tau_{X}^{2}\wedge\tau_{Y}^{2}}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
;

•

\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{2\tau_{X}^{2}}\tr(H_{[11]}\circ G_{[12]})\vee\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{2\tau_{Y}^{2}}\tr(H_{[22]}\circ G_{[12]})\lesssim_{M}\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{3}}{\tau_{X}\wedge\tau_{Y}}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
;

•

\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}}{\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}}\tr(H_{[11]}\circ H_{[22]})\lesssim\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}}{\tau_{X}\tau_{Y}}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
.

Collecting the bounds we conclude that
\mathbb{E}\bar{g}_{1}^{2}(X,Y)\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-2}\tau_{Y}^{-2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
.

(2). When
X=Y
, we may write with
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{p})
that

\displaystyle\bar{g}_{1}(Z)=\bar{g}_{1}(X,Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1}{2\tau_{X}^{2}}\cdot\Big[Z^{\top}\bar{A}Z-\mathbb{E}(Z^{\top}\bar{A}Z)\Big],

with the symmetric matrix
\bar{A}
defined by
\bar{A}\equiv\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\Big[\Sigma_{X}-\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}/\tau_{X}^{2}\Big]\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}
. Using a similar argument as in the previous case,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bar{g}_{1}^{2}(X,Y)

\displaystyle\asymp\tau_{X}^{-4}\bigg\lVert\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\Big[\Sigma_{X}-\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}/\tau_{X}^{2}\Big]\Sigma_{X}^{1/2}\bigg\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-4}\big\lVert\Sigma_{X}-\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}/\tau_{X}^{2}\big\rVert_{F}^{2}=\tau_{X}^{-4}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{4}p/\tau_{X}^{4}.

A matching upper bound can be easily proved under the condition
\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{\op}\leq M
. ∎

D.5.
Proof of Lemma
9.6

We only prove the case for
\bar{\psi}_{X}
. In the definition (
9.1
) of
\bar{\psi}_{X}(X,Y)
, we write
\bar{\psi}_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})\equiv\bar{A}_{1,X}(x_{1},y_{1})+A_{2,X}(x_{1},y_{1})
. Then we have

\displaystyle\var\big(\bar{\psi}_{X}(X,Y)\big)\lesssim\var\big(\bar{A}_{1,X}(X,Y))+\var\big(A_{2,X}(X,Y)\big).

Let
(X^{\top},Y^{\top})^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\Sigma^{1/2}Z
. We may write

\displaystyle\bar{A}_{1,X}(X,Y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\tau_{X}^{4}}\bigg[\big(\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}-\tr(\Sigma_{X})\big)(X^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y-\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2})+2X^{\top}\Sigma_{X}\Sigma_{XY}Y\bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\frac{1}{2\tau_{X}^{4}}\bigg[\big(Z^{\top}H_{[11]}Z-\tr(H_{[11]})\big)\big(Z^{\top}\bar{G}_{[1,2]}Z-\tr(\bar{G}_{[1,2]})\big)+2Z^{\top}\bar{G}_{X}Z\bigg],

with
\bar{G}_{X}
is defined in (
C.14
). By the Poincaré inequality, we have

\displaystyle\var\big(\tau_{X}^{4}\bar{A}_{1,X}(X,Y)\big)=\var\big(\tau_{X}^{4}\bar{A}_{1,X}(Z)\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\big(Z^{\top}H_{[11]}Z-\tr(H_{[11]})\big)^{2}Z^{\top}\bar{G}_{[1,2]}^{2}Z+\mathbb{E}\big(Z^{\top}\bar{G}_{[1,2]}Z-\tr(\bar{G}_{[1,2]})\big)^{2}Z^{\top}G_{[11]}Z+\mathbb{E}Z^{\top}\bar{G}_{X}^{2}Z

\displaystyle\lesssim\lVert G_{[12]}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert H_{[11]}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\lVert\bar{G}_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2},

On the other hand, by the estimate in (
C.16
),

\displaystyle\var\big(A_{2,X}(X,Y)\big)\lesssim\mathbb{E}A_{2,X}^{2}(X,Y)\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-6}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

Collecting the bounds to conclude.∎

D.6.
Proof of Lemma
9.8

As
\mathbb{E}_{Z_{3},Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})=0
, Proposition
2.1
yields that

\displaystyle k_{2}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4!}\sum_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4)}\bigg[\mathbb{E}_{Z_{3},Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2\mathbb{E}_{Z_{3},Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{3}})\bigg].

The claim now follows by calculating

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4)}\mathbb{E}_{Z_{3},Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}})

\displaystyle=4U(x_{1},x_{2})V(y_{1},y_{2})+8\mathbb{E}U(x_{1},X)V(y_{1},Y)+8\mathbb{E}U(x_{2},X)V(y_{2},Y)+4\dcov^{2}(X,Y),

and

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4)}\mathbb{E}_{Z_{3},Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{3}})

\displaystyle=\bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4),\\
i_{2}=1,i_{3}=2\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4),\\
i_{2}=2,i_{3}=1\end{subarray}}\bigg]\mathbb{E}_{Z_{3},Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{3}})

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}U(x_{1},X)V(y_{2},Y)+2\mathbb{E}U(x_{2},X)V(y_{1},Y),

as desired.∎

D.7.
Proof of Lemma
9.9

By definition of
g_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle g_{2}\big((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2})\big)

\displaystyle\equiv k_{2}\big((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2})\big)-k_{1}(x_{1},y_{1})-k_{1}(x_{2},y_{2})+\dcov^{2}(X,Y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\bigg[U(x_{1},x_{2})V(y_{1},y_{2})-\mathbb{E}U(x_{1},X)V(y_{1},Y)-\mathbb{E}U(x_{2},X)V(y_{2},Y)+\dcov^{2}(X,Y)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}U(x_{1},X)V(y_{2},Y)-\mathbb{E}U(x_{2},X)V(y_{1},Y)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6\tau_{X}\tau_{Y}}\bigg[\Big(x_{1}^{\top}x_{2}y_{1}^{\top}y_{2}-x_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}y_{1}-x_{2}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}y_{2}+\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\Big)-\Big(x_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}y_{2}+x_{2}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}y_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\big[\bar{R}_{2}\big((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2})\big)-\mathbb{E}\bar{R}_{2}\big((X_{1},Y_{1}),(X_{2},Y_{2})\big)\big]\bigg],

completing the proof.∎

D.8.
Proof of Lemma
9.11

As
\mathbb{E}_{Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})=0
, Proposition
2.1
yields that

\displaystyle k_{3}(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4!}\sum_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4)}\bigg[\mathbb{E}_{Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}})-2\mathbb{E}_{Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{3}})\bigg].

The claim follows by calculating

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4)}\mathbb{E}_{Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}})

\displaystyle=\bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4)\\
i_{1},i_{2}\neq 4\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4)\\
i_{1}=4\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4)\\
i_{2}=4\end{subarray}}\bigg]\mathbb{E}_{Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}})

\displaystyle=2\sum_{1\leq i_{1}\neq i_{2}\leq 3}U(x_{i_{1}},x_{i_{2}})V(y_{i_{1}},y_{i_{2}})+4\sum_{1\leq i\leq 3}\mathbb{E}U(X,x_{i})V(Y,y_{i}),

and

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4)}\mathbb{E}_{Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{3}})

\displaystyle=\bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4),\\
i_{1}=4\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4),\\
i_{4}=4\end{subarray}}\bigg]\mathbb{E}_{Z_{4}}U(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{3}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq i_{1}\neq i_{2}\leq 3}\mathbb{E}U(X,x_{i_{1}})V(Y,y_{i_{2}})+\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3})\in\sigma(1,2,3)}U(x_{i_{1}},x_{i_{2}})V(y_{i_{1}},y_{i_{3}}),

as desired.∎

Appendix E
Proofs for Section
