.

(
Variance of
T_{4}
and
T_{5}
) By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\var(T_{4})\asymp n^{5}\var\big(X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}Y_{1}\big)\equiv n^{5}\var(U).

Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert\nabla_{X_{1}}(X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{X}\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}Y_{1})\rVert^{2}=\mathbb{E}\lVert\nabla_{X_{1}}(\Sigma_{X}\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}Y_{1})\rVert^{2}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

A similar bound holds for the gradient with respect to
Y_{1}
. By Gaussian-Poincaré inequality and a change of variable, we then have

\displaystyle\var(T_{4})\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

A similar bound holds for
\var(T_{5})
.

(
Variance of
T_{6}
) Recall the notation
U_{k}\equiv H_{[11]}Z_{k}Z_{k}^{\top}H_{[22]}-G_{[12]}
. Then

\displaystyle T_{6}

\displaystyle=(n-1)\sum_{k\neq\ell}(Z_{k}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{k}-\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2})\cdot(Z_{\ell}^{\top}U_{\ell}Z_{\ell}).

Then for any
i\in[n]
and
j\in[p+q]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial T_{6}}{\partial Z_{ij}}

\displaystyle=(n-1)\cdot\sum_{k\neq\ell}\bigg[\delta_{ki}(Z_{k}^{\top}G_{[12]}e_{j}+e_{j}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{k})\cdot(Z_{\ell}^{\top}U_{k}Z_{\ell})

\displaystyle\quad+\delta_{\ell i}(Z_{k}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{k}-\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2})(e_{j}^{\top}U_{k}Z_{\ell}+Z_{\ell}^{\top}U_{k}e_{j})

\displaystyle\quad+\delta_{ki}(Z_{k}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{k}-\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2})\cdot Z_{\ell}^{\top}\big[H_{[11]}(Z_{k}e_{j}^{\top}+e_{j}Z_{k}^{\top})H_{[22]}\big]Z_{\ell}\bigg]

\displaystyle=(n-1)\cdot\sum_{k\neq i}\bigg[(e_{j}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{i})(Z_{k}^{\top}U_{i}Z_{k})+(Z_{i}^{\top}G_{[12]}e_{j})(Z_{k}^{\top}U_{i}Z_{k})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(Z_{k}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{k}-\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2})(e_{j}^{\top}U_{k}Z_{i})+(Z_{k}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{k}-\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2})(Z_{i}^{\top}U_{k}e_{j})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(Z_{i}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{i}-\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2})(Z_{k}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{i}e_{j}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{k})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(Z_{i}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{i}-\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2})(Z_{k}^{\top}H_{[11]}e_{j}Z_{i}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{k})\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv(n-1)\cdot(T_{61}+T_{62}+T_{63}+T_{64}+T_{65}+T_{66})_{ij}.

For
T_{61}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert T_{61}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=n\cdot\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\neq 1}(Z_{k_{1}}^{\top}U_{1}Z_{k_{1}})(Z_{k_{2}}^{\top}U_{1}Z_{k_{2}})(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[21]}G_{[12]}Z_{1})

\displaystyle\asymp n\cdot\Big[n\cdot\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}U_{1}Z_{2})^{2}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}G_{[21]}Z_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+n^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}U_{1}Z_{2})(Z_{3}^{\top}U_{1}Z_{3})(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}G_{[21]}Z_{1})\Big].

The two terms can be bounded as follows:

•

By Lemma
E.2
-(4), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}U_{1}Z_{2})^{2}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}G_{[21]}Z_{1})\leq\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Z_{2}^{\top}U_{1}Z_{2})^{4}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}G_{[21]}Z_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\big[\tr^{2}(U_{1})+\lVert U_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}\big]\cdot\lVert G_{[12]}\rVert_{F}^{2}\asymp_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}.

•

Again by Lemma
E.2
-(4), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}U_{1}Z_{2})(Z_{3}^{\top}U_{1}Z_{3})(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}G_{[21]}Z_{1})=\mathbb{E}\tr(U_{1})^{2}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}G_{[21]}Z_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\tr(U_{1})^{4}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}G_{[21]}Z_{1})^{2}\lesssim\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}=\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}.

In summary we have

(E.11)

\displaystyle(n-1)\cdot\mathbb{E}\lVert T_{61}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim_{M}n^{3}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}+n^{4}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}.

A similar bound holds for
T_{62}
.

For
T_{63}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert T_{63}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\sum_{i}\bigg\lVert\sum_{k\neq i}(Z_{k}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{k}-\tr(G_{[12]}))U_{k}Z_{i}\bigg\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=n\cdot\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\neq 1}\mathbb{E}(Z_{k_{1}}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{k_{1}}-\tr(G_{[12]}))(Z_{k_{2}}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{k_{2}}-\tr(G_{[12]}))(Z_{1}^{\top}U_{k_{1}}^{\top}U_{k_{2}}Z_{1})

\displaystyle\asymp n\cdot\Big[n\cdot\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[21]}Z_{2}-\tr(G_{[12]}))^{2}\lVert U_{2}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+n^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{2}-\tr(G_{[12]}))(Z_{3}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{3}-\tr(G_{[12]}))\tr(U_{2}^{\top}U_{3})\Big].

The two terms can be bounded as follows:

•

By Lemma
E.2
-(4),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[21]}Z_{2}-\tr(G_{[12]}))^{2}\lVert U_{2}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[21]}Z_{2}-\tr(G_{[12]}))^{4}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\lVert U_{2}\rVert_{F}^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\lVert G_{[12]}\rVert_{F}^{2}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{2}=\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

•

By Lemma
E.2
-(4)(5), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{2}-\tr(G_{[12]}))(Z_{3}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{3}-\tr(G_{[12]}))\tr(U_{2}^{\top}U_{3})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}^{1/4}(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{2}-\tr(G_{[12]}))^{4}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/4}(Z_{3}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{3}-\tr(G_{[12]}))^{4}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}\tr^{2}(U_{2}^{\top}U_{3})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

In summary, we have

(E.12)

\displaystyle(n-1)\cdot\mathbb{E}\lVert T_{63}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}n^{4}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

A similar bound holds for
T_{64}
.

For
T_{65}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert T_{65}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}\bigg\lVert\sum_{k\neq i}(Z_{i}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{i}-\tr(G_{[12]}))(Z_{k}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{i})H_{[22]}Z_{k}\bigg\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=n\cdot\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\neq 1}\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{1}-\tr(G_{[12]}))^{2}(Z_{k_{1}}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{1})(Z_{k_{2}}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{1})(Z_{k_{1}}^{\top}G_{[22]}Z_{k_{2}})

\displaystyle\asymp n\cdot\Big[n\cdot\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{1}-\tr(G_{[12]}))^{2}(Z_{2}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{1})^{2}(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[22]}Z_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+n^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{1}-\tr(G_{[12]}))^{2}(Z_{2}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{1})(Z_{3}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{1})(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[22]}Z_{3})\Big].

The two terms can be bounded as follows

•

By first taking expectation with respect to
Z_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{1}-\tr(G_{[12]}))^{2}(Z_{2}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{1})^{2}(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[22]}Z_{2})

\displaystyle\asymp\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{1}-\tr(G_{[12]}))^{2}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[11]}Z_{1})\tr(G_{[22]})

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{1}-\tr(G_{[12]}))^{4}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[11]}Z_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

•

By taking expectation with respect to
Z_{2},Z_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{1}-\tr(G_{[12]}))^{2}(Z_{2}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{1})(Z_{3}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{1})(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[22]}Z_{3})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{1}-\tr(G_{[12]}))^{4}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Z_{1}^{\top}H_{[11]}G_{[22]}H_{[11]}Z_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}=\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}.

In summary we have

(E.13)

\displaystyle(n-1)\cdot\mathbb{E}\lVert T_{65}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim_{M}n^{3}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}+n^{4}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}.

A similar bound holds for
T_{64}
. Combining (
E.11
) - (
E.13
) and Lemma
G.5
concludes the proof.

(
Variance of
T_{7}
) Note that
T_{7}
can be decomposed as
T_{7}=\kappa(T_{7,1}+T_{7,2}+T_{7,3})
, where

\displaystyle T_{7,1}

\displaystyle\equiv(n-1)^{2}\tr\Big(\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}0&\sum_{j=1}^{n}X_{j}Y_{j}^{\top}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2}\circ G_{[12]}\Big),

\displaystyle T_{7,2}

\displaystyle\equiv(n-2)\sum_{j,k}\tr\Big(\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}0&X_{j}Y_{j}^{\top}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2}\circ\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}0&X_{k}Y_{k}^{\top}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2}\Big),

\displaystyle T_{7,3}

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{j=1}^{n}\tr\Big(\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}0&X_{j}Y_{j}^{\top}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2}\circ\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}0&X_{j}Y_{j}^{\top}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2}\Big).

To control the variance of
T_{7,1}
, note that

\displaystyle\var(T_{7,1})\asymp n^{5}\var\Big(\tr\Big(\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}0&XY^{\top}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2}\circ G_{[12]}\Big)\Big)=n^{5}\cdot\var(f(Z)),

where
f(Z)\equiv\tr\big(H_{[11]}ZZ^{\top}H_{[22]}\circ G_{[12]}\big)=\sum_{i}(e_{i}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z)(e_{i}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z)(G_{[12]})_{ii}
. Using
\nabla f(Z)=(A_{1}+A_{2})Z
with
A_{1}\equiv\sum_{i}H_{[11]}e_{i}e_{i}^{\top}H_{[22]}(G_{[12]})_{ii}
and
A_{2}\equiv\sum_{i}H_{[22]}e_{i}e_{i}^{\top}H_{[11]}(G_{[12]})_{ii}
, it suffices to bound
\lVert A_{1}\rVert_{F}
and
\lVert A_{2}\rVert_{F}
so that by the Poincaré inequality,
\var(T_{7,1})\lesssim n^{5}(\lVert A_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}\vee\lVert A_{2}\rVert_{F}^{2})
. To bound
\lVert A_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}
, note that
(A_{1})_{k\ell}=\sum_{i}(H_{11})_{ki}(H_{22})_{\ell i}(G_{[12]})_{ii}
, so that

\displaystyle\lVert A_{1}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,\ell}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}}(H_{[11]})_{k,i_{1}}(H_{[11]})_{k,i_{2}}(H_{[22]})_{\ell,i_{1}}(H_{[22]})_{\ell,i_{2}}(G_{[12]})_{i_{1},i_{1}}(G_{[12]})_{i_{2},i_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}(G_{[11]})_{ij}(G_{[22]})_{ij}(G_{[12]})_{ii}(G_{[12]})_{jj}\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i,j}(G_{[11]})^{2}_{ij}(G_{[12]})^{2}_{ii}}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{i,j}(G_{[22]})^{2}_{ij}(G_{[12]})^{2}_{jj}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{i}(G_{[11]}^{2})_{ii}(G_{[12]})^{2}_{ii}}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{i}(G^{2}_{[22]})_{ii}(G_{[12]})^{2}_{ii}}\lesssim_{M}\lVert G_{[12]}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

A similar bound holds for
\lVert A_{2}\rVert_{F}^{2}
so we have
\var(T_{7,1})\lesssim n^{5}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
.

Next we bound
\var(T_{7,2})
. Using again
\Sigma^{1/2}[0,XY^{\top};0,0]\Sigma^{1/2}=H_{[11]}ZZ^{\top}H_{[22]}
, we have

\displaystyle\var(T_{7,2})\asymp n^{2}\cdot\var\Big(\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}(e_{i}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{j})(e_{i}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{j})\Big)^{2}\Big)\equiv n^{2}\var(f(Z))

for
Z=[Z_{1}^{\top},\ldots,Z_{n}^{\top}]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times(p+q)}
. Using

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f(Z)}{\partial Z_{k}}=\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}(e_{i}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{j})(e_{i}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{j})\Big)(H_{[11]}e_{i}e_{i}^{\top}H_{[22]}+H_{[22]}e_{i}e_{i}^{\top}H_{[11]})Z_{k},

we have by symmetry (and with
M_{i}\equiv H_{[11]}e_{i}e_{i}^{\top}H_{[22]}
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert\nabla f(Z)\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\sum_{k}\lVert\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}(e_{i}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{j})(e_{i}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{j})\Big)H_{[11]}e_{i}e_{i}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{k}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=n\cdot\mathbb{E}\lVert\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}(e_{i}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{j})(e_{i}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{j})\Big)H_{[11]}e_{i}e_{i}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{1}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv n\cdot\mathbb{E}\lVert\sum_{i}\Big(\sum_{j}Z_{j}^{\top}M_{i}\Big)M_{i}Z_{1}\rVert^{2}=n\cdot\mathbb{E}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}}\Big(\sum_{j}Z_{j}^{\top}M_{i_{1}}Z_{j}\Big)\Big(\sum_{j}Z_{j}^{\top}M_{i_{2}}Z_{j}\Big)Z_{1}^{\top}M_{i_{1}}^{\top}M_{i_{2}}Z_{1}

\displaystyle=n\cdot\Big[\mathbb{E}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}}(Z_{1}^{\top}M_{i_{1}}Z_{1})(Z_{1}^{\top}M_{i_{2}}Z_{1})(Z_{1}^{\top}M_{i_{1}}^{\top}M_{i_{2}}Z_{1})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+(2n-1)\mathbb{E}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}}(Z_{1}^{\top}M_{i_{1}}Z_{1})(Z_{2}^{\top}M_{i_{2}}Z_{2})(Z_{1}^{\top}M_{i_{1}}^{\top}M_{i_{2}}Z_{1})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+(n^{2}-2n)\mathbb{E}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}}(Z_{2}^{\top}M_{i_{1}}Z_{2})(Z_{3}^{\top}M_{i_{2}}Z_{3})(Z_{1}^{\top}M_{i_{1}}^{\top}M_{i_{2}}Z_{1})\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}n\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{4}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{4}+n^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{2}+n^{3}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

Here the last inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwarz and some simple estimates based on Lemma
G.3
. Along with the Poincaré inequality, this concludes the desired variance bound for
T_{7,2}
. The variance bound for
T_{7,3}
is straightforward so we omit it.
∎

Lemma E.2
.

Let
U\equiv H_{[11]}ZZ^{\top}H_{[22]}-G_{[12]}
with
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{p+q})
, and
U_{1},U_{2},\ldots
be independent copies of
U
. Then the following hold.

(1)

\mathbb{E}U^{2}=G_{[11]}G_{[22]}+\tr(G_{[12]})G_{[12]}+\kappa H_{[11]}(I\circ G_{[21]})H_{[22]}
.

(2)

\mathbb{E}U^{\top}U=G_{[21]}G_{[12]}+G_{[22]}\tr(G_{[11]})+\kappa H_{[11]}(I\circ G_{[21]})H_{[22]}
,
\mathbb{E}UU^{\top}=G_{[12]}G_{[21]}+G_{[11]}\tr(G_{[22]})+\kappa H_{[11]}(I\circ G_{[22]})H_{[11]}
.

(3)

\mathbb{E}\tr(U)U=G_{[11]}G_{[22]}+G_{[12]}^{2}+\kappa H_{[11]}(I\circ G_{[12]})H_{[22]}
.

(4)

\mathbb{E}\tr^{4}(U)\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}
and
\mathbb{E}\lVert U\rVert_{F}^{4}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{4}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{4}
.

(5)

\mathbb{E}\tr^{2}(U_{1}^{\top}U_{2})\lesssim\lVert\Sigma_{X}^{2}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}^{2}\rVert_{F}^{2}
,
\mathbb{E}\tr^{2}(U_{1}U_{2})\lesssim\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\rVert_{F}^{4}
.

(6)

\mathbb{E}\tr(U_{1})\tr(U_{2})\tr(U_{1}^{\top}U_{2})\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
.

Proof.

(1). By Lemma
G.3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}U^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big(H_{[11]}ZZ^{\top}H_{[22]}H_{[11]}ZZ^{\top}H_{[22]}\big)-G_{[12]}^{2}

\displaystyle=H_{[11]}\cdot\mathbb{E}(ZZ^{\top})(Z^{\top}H_{[22]}H_{[11]}Z)\cdot H_{[22]}-G^{2}_{[12]}

\displaystyle=H_{[11]}\big(H_{[11]}H_{[22]}+H_{[22]}H_{[11]}+\tr(G_{[12]})\cdot I+\kappa I\circ G_{[21]}\big)H_{[22]}-G^{2}_{[12]}

\displaystyle=G_{[11]}G_{[22]}+\tr(G_{[12]})G_{[12]}+\kappa H_{[11]}(I\circ G_{[21]})H_{[22]}.

(2). By Lemma
G.3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}U^{\top}U

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big(H_{[22]}ZZ^{\top}H_{[11]}^{2}ZZ^{\top}H_{[22]}\big)-G_{[21]}G_{[12]}

\displaystyle=H_{[22]}\cdot\mathbb{E}(ZZ^{\top})(Z^{\top}H_{[11]}^{2}Z)\cdot H_{[22]}-G_{[21]}G_{[12]}

\displaystyle=H_{[22]}\big(2H_{[11]}^{2}+\tr(G_{[11]})\cdot I+\kappa I\circ G_{[11]}\big)H_{[22]}-G_{[21]}G_{[12]}

\displaystyle=G_{[21]}G_{[12]}+G_{[22]}\tr(G_{[11]})+\kappa H_{[11]}(I\circ G_{[21]})H_{[22]},

and similarly

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}UU^{\top}

\displaystyle=G_{[12]}G_{[21]}+G_{[11]}\tr(G_{[22]})+\kappa H_{[11]}(I\circ G_{[22]})H_{[11]}.

(3). We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\tr(U)U

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(Z^{\top}G_{[12]}Z-\tr(G_{[12]})\Big)\Big(H_{[11]}ZZ^{\top}H_{[22]}-G_{[12]}\Big)

\displaystyle=H_{[11]}\cdot\mathbb{E}(ZZ^{\top})(Z^{\top}G_{[12]}Z)\cdot H_{[22]}-\tr(G_{[12]})G_{[12]}

\displaystyle=H_{[11]}\big(H_{[11]}H_{[22]}+H_{[22]}H_{[11]}+\tr(G_{[12]})\cdot I+\kappa I\circ G_{[12]}\big)H_{[22]}-\tr(G_{[12]})G_{[12]}

\displaystyle=G_{[11]}G_{[22]}+G_{[12]}^{2}+\kappa H_{[11]}(I\circ G_{[12]})H_{[22]}.

(4). We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\tr(U)^{4}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[Z^{\top}G_{[12]}Z-\mathbb{E}(Z^{\top}G_{[12]}Z)\big]^{4}\lesssim\lVert G_{[12]}\rVert_{F}^{4}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert U\rVert_{F}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\lVert H_{[11]}ZZ^{\top}H_{[22]}\rVert_{F}^{4}+\lVert G_{[12]}\rVert_{F}^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\mathbb{E}\lVert H_{[11]}Z\rVert^{4}\lVert H_{[22]}Z\rVert^{4}+\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}\lesssim\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{4}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{4}+\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}.

The claim follows by Lemma
G.5
.

(5). By definition, we have

\displaystyle\tr(U_{1}^{\top}U_{2})

\displaystyle=\tr\bigg(\big[H_{[22]}Z_{1}Z_{1}^{\top}H_{[11]}-G_{[21]}\big]\big[H_{[11]}Z_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}H_{[22]}-G_{[12]}\big]\bigg)

\displaystyle=(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[11]}Z_{2})(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[22]}Z_{2})-Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[11]}G_{[22]}Z_{1}-Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[11]}G_{[22]}Z_{2}+\tr(G_{[11]}G_{[22]})

\displaystyle=Z_{1}^{\top}\Big(G_{[11]}Z_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[22]}-G_{[11]}G_{[22]}\Big)Z_{1}-\tr(G_{[11]}Z_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[22]}-G_{[11]}G_{[22]})

\displaystyle\equiv Z_{1}^{\top}A(Z_{2})Z_{1}-\mathbb{E}_{1}(Z_{1}^{\top}A(Z_{2})Z_{1}),

where
\mathbb{E}_{i}
denotes expectation only with respect to
Z_{i}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\tr^{2}(U_{1}^{\top}U_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{2}\var_{1}(Z_{1}^{\top}A(Z_{2})Z_{1})\asymp\mathbb{E}\lVert A(Z)\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\lVert G_{[11]}ZZ^{\top}G_{[22]}-G_{[11]}G_{[22]}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[(Z^{\top}G_{[11]}^{2}Z)(Z^{\top}G_{[22]}^{2}Z)-2Z^{\top}G_{[11]}^{2}G_{[22]}^{2}Z+\tr(G_{[11]}^{2}G_{[22]}^{2})\Big]

\displaystyle=\tr(G_{[11]}^{2}G_{[22]}^{2})+\lVert G_{[11]}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert G_{[22]}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\kappa\tr(G_{[11]}^{2}\circ G_{[22]}^{2})

\displaystyle\asymp_{M}\lVert G_{[11]}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert G_{[22]}\rVert_{F}^{2}=\lVert\Sigma_{X}^{2}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}^{2}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

On the other hand, as

\displaystyle\tr(U_{1}U_{2})

\displaystyle=\tr\bigg(\big[H_{[11]}Z_{1}Z_{1}^{\top}H_{[22]}-G_{[12]}\big]\big[H_{[11]}Z_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}H_{[22]}-G_{[12]}\big]\bigg)

\displaystyle=(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{2})(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[21]}Z_{2})-Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}^{2}Z_{1}-Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[12]}^{2}Z_{2}+\tr(G_{[12]}^{2})

\displaystyle=Z_{1}^{\top}\Big(G_{[12]}Z_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[12]}-G_{[12]}^{2}\Big)Z_{1}-\tr(G_{[12]}Z_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[12]}-G_{[12]}^{2})

\displaystyle\equiv Z_{1}^{\top}B(Z_{2})Z_{1}-\mathbb{E}_{1}(Z_{1}^{\top}B(Z_{2})Z_{1}),

so using a similar argument as in the case for
\mathbb{E}\tr^{2}(U_{1}^{\top}U_{2})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\tr^{2}(U_{1}U_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\lVert G_{[12]}ZZ^{\top}G_{[12]}-G_{[12]}^{2}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[(Z^{\top}G_{[12]}G_{[21]}Z)(Z^{\top}G_{[21]}G_{[12]}Z)-2Z^{\top}G_{[12]}G_{[21]}^{2}G_{[12]}Z+\tr(G_{[12]}^{2}G_{[21]}^{2})\Big]

\displaystyle=\tr(G_{[12]}^{2}G_{[21]}^{2})+\lVert G_{[12]}\rVert_{F}^{4}+\kappa\tr(G_{[12]}G_{[21]}\circ G_{[21]}G_{[12]})\lesssim\lVert G_{[12]}\rVert_{F}^{4}=\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\rVert_{F}^{4}.

(6). Using the formula for
\tr(U_{1}^{\top}U_{2})
in the proof of (5) above,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\tr(U_{1})\tr(U_{2})\tr(U_{1}^{\top}U_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{1}-\tr(G_{[12]}))(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{2}-\tr(G_{[12]}))(Z_{1}^{\top}A(Z_{2})Z_{1}-\tr(A(Z_{2})))

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{2}-\tr(G_{[12]}))\cdot\big[\tr(G_{[12]}A(Z_{2})+G_{[12]}A(Z_{2})^{\top})+\kappa\tr(G_{[12]}\circ A(Z_{2}))\big]\quad\hbox{(by Lemma \ref{lem:gaussian_4moment}-(1))}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{2}-\tr(G_{[12]}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\cdot\big[Z_{2}^{\top}\big(G_{[11]}(G_{[12]}+G_{[21]})G_{[22]}\big)Z_{2}-\tr\big(G_{[11]}(G_{[12]}+G_{[21]})G_{[22]}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\kappa\cdot\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{2}-\tr(G_{[12]}))\big(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[11]}(I\circ G_{[12]})G_{[22]}Z_{2}-\tr(G_{[11]}(I\circ G_{[12]})G_{[22]})\big)

\displaystyle\asymp\tr\big[G_{[12]}\cdot\big(G_{[11]}(G_{[12]}+G_{[21]})G_{[22]}\big)+\kappa G_{[12]}\circ\big(G_{[11]}(G_{[12]}+G_{[21]})G_{[22]}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\kappa\cdot\tr\big[(G_{[12]}\cdot G_{[11]}(I\circ G_{[12]})G_{[22]}+\kappa\cdot G_{[12]}\circ(G_{[11]}(I\circ G_{[12]})G_{[22]})\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\tr\big[(\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX})^{3}\big]+\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\asymp_{M}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

The proof is complete.
∎

E.2.2.
Third moment bound

Proof of Proposition
10.4
-(2).

For any
i\in[n]
, let
M_{-i}\equiv\sum_{k\neq i}X_{k}Y_{k}^{\top}
. By definition of
\Delta_{i}\psi_{1}(\bm{X},\bm{Y})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\Delta_{i}\psi_{1}(\bm{X},\bm{Y})|^{3}\lesssim\mathbb{E}^{3/4}(X_{i}^{\top}M_{-i}Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime\top}M_{-i}Y_{i}^{\prime})^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}^{3/4}\big[X_{i}^{\top}M_{-i}Y_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{(X_{i},Y_{i})}(X_{i}^{\top}M_{-i}Y_{i})\big]^{4}+\mathbb{E}^{3/4}\big[X_{i}^{\prime\top}M_{-i}Y_{i}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}_{(X_{i}^{\prime},Y_{i}^{\prime})}(X_{i}^{\prime\top}M_{-i}Y_{i}^{\prime})\big]^{4}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{3/4}\big[Z_{i}^{\top}\widetilde{M}_{-i}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{Z_{i}}(Z_{i}^{\top}\widetilde{M}_{-i}Z_{i})\big]^{4}+\mathbb{E}^{3/4}\big[Z_{i}^{\prime\top}\widetilde{M}_{-i}Z_{i}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}_{Z_{i}^{\prime}}(Z_{i}^{\prime\top}M_{-i}Z_{i}^{\prime})\big]^{4}.

Here
\widetilde{M}_{-i}
is the random matrix defined by

\displaystyle\widetilde{M}_{-i}\equiv\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}0&M_{-i}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2}.

Hence we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\Delta_{i}\psi_{1}(\bm{X},\bm{Y})|^{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}^{3/4}\lVert\widetilde{M}_{-i}\rVert_{F}^{4}\lesssim_{M}\mathbb{E}^{3/4}\lVert M_{-i}\rVert_{F}^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}n^{3/2}\tr^{3/2}(\Sigma_{X})\tr^{3/2}(\Sigma_{Y})+n^{3}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{3},

using Lemma
E.3
in the last step.
∎

Lemma E.3
.

For any
i\in[n]
, let
M_{-i}\equiv\sum_{k\neq i}X_{k}Y_{k}^{\top}
. Then the mean and variance of
\lVert M_{-i}\rVert_{F}^{2}
satisfy:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert M_{-i}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=n(n-1)\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}+(n-1)\tr(\Sigma_{X})\tr(\Sigma_{Y})+\kappa(n-1)\tr(H_{[11]}\circ H_{[22]}),

\displaystyle\var\big(\lVert M_{-i}\rVert_{F}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}n\cdot\big[\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}(n\vee\tau_{X}^{2}\vee\tau_{Y}^{2})+n^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\big].

Consequently,
\mathbb{E}\lVert M_{-i}\rVert_{F}^{4}\lesssim n^{2}\tr^{2}(\Sigma_{X})\tr^{2}(\Sigma_{Y})+n^{4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we let
i=1
. Note that

\displaystyle\lVert M_{-1}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\neq 1}(X_{k_{1}}^{\top}X_{k_{2}})(Y_{k_{1}}^{\top}Y_{k_{2}})=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\neq 1}(Z_{k_{1}}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{k_{2}})(Z_{k_{1}}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{k_{2}}).

Hence the mean formula is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert M_{-1}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=(n-1)\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{1})(Z_{1}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{1})+(n-1)(n-2)\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{2})(Z_{1}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{2})

\displaystyle=(n-1)\cdot\big[2\tr(G_{[12]})+\tr(H_{[11]})\tr(H_{[22]})+\kappa\tr(H_{[11]}\circ H_{[22]})\big]+(n-1)(n-2)\tr(G_{[12]})

\displaystyle=n(n-1)\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}+(n-1)\tr(\Sigma_{X})\tr(\Sigma_{Y})+\kappa(n-1)\tr(H_{[11]}\circ H_{[22]}).

To bound the variance of
\lVert M_{-1}\rVert_{F}^{2}
, write

\displaystyle T(Z)\equiv\lVert M_{-1}\rVert_{F}^{2}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\neq 1}(Z_{k_{1}}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{k_{2}})(Z_{k_{1}}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{k_{2}}).

So for any
i\neq 1
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{Z_{i}}T(Z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\neq 1}\Big[(\delta_{k_{1},i}\cdot H_{[11]}Z_{k_{2}}+\delta_{k_{2},i}\cdot H_{[11]}Z_{k_{1}})(Z_{k_{1}}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{k_{2}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(\delta_{k_{1},i}\cdot H_{[22]}Z_{k_{2}}+\delta_{k_{2},i}H_{[22]}Z_{k_{1}})(Z_{k_{1}}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{k_{2}})\Big]

\displaystyle=2\cdot\sum_{k\neq 1}(Z_{k}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{i})H_{[11]}Z_{k}+2\cdot\sum_{k\neq 1}(Z_{k}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{i})H_{[22]}Z_{k}\equiv[T_{11}+T_{12}]_{i}.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert T_{11}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\asymp n\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg\lVert\sum_{k\neq 1}(Z_{k}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{2})H_{[11]}Z_{k}\bigg\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=n\cdot\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\neq 1}\mathbb{E}(Z_{k_{1}}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{2})(Z_{k_{2}}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{2})(Z_{k_{1}}^{\top}G_{[11]}Z_{k_{2}})

\displaystyle\asymp n\cdot\Big[\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{2})^{2}(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[11]}Z_{2})+n\cdot\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{2})(Z_{3}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{2})(Z_{2}^{\top}G_{[11]}Z_{3})

\displaystyle\quad+n\cdot\mathbb{E}(Z_{3}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{2})^{2}(Z_{3}^{\top}G_{[11]}Z_{3})+n^{2}\mathbb{E}(Z_{3}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{2})(Z_{4}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{2})(Z_{3}^{\top}G_{[11]}Z_{4})\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}n(\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{4}+n\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}+n^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}).

Flipping
X
,
Y
for
T_{12}
to conclude that

\displaystyle\lVert\nabla_{Z}T(Z)\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim_{M}n\cdot\big[\tau_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}(n\vee\tau_{X}^{2}\vee\tau_{Y}^{2})+n^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\big],

completing the proof.
∎

E.3.
Proof of Proposition
10.5

We only prove the claim for the term
X_{i}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{j}
, the bound for the other term is completely analogous. With some abuse of notation, we write in the proof
\psi_{2}(\bm{X},\bm{Y})\equiv\sum_{i\neq j}X_{i}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{j}
.

(1). For any
i\in[n]
, as

\displaystyle\psi_{2}(\bm{X},\bm{Y})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\neq i_{2}\\
i_{1},i_{2}\neq i\end{subarray}}X_{i_{1}}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{i_{2}}+\sum_{i_{1}\neq i}X_{i_{1}}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{i}+\sum_{i_{2}\neq i}X_{i}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{i_{2}},

we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}\psi_{2}(\bm{X},\bm{Y})

\displaystyle\equiv\psi_{2}(\bm{X},\bm{Y})-\psi_{2}(\bm{X}^{\{i\}},\bm{Y}^{\{i\}})

\displaystyle=\Big(\sum_{j\neq i}X_{j}\Big)^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}(Y_{i}-Y_{i}^{\prime})+(X_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}\Big(\sum_{j\neq i}Y_{j}\Big).

Hence for any
A\subset[n]
such that
i\notin A
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}\psi_{2}(\bm{X}^{A},\bm{Y}^{A})

\displaystyle=\Big(\sum_{j\in A}X_{j}^{\prime}+\sum_{j\notin A\cup\{i\}}X_{j}\Big)^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}(Y_{i}-Y_{i}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+(X_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}\Big(\sum_{j\in A}Y_{j}^{\prime}+\sum_{j\notin A\cup\{i\}}Y_{j}\Big).

This implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\big[\Delta_{i}\psi_{2}(\bm{X},\bm{Y})\Delta_{i}\psi_{2}(\bm{X}^{A},\bm{Y}^{A})\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j\neq i}X_{j}\Big)^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}(Y_{i}-Y_{i}^{\prime})\cdot\Big(\sum_{j\in A}X_{j}^{\prime}+\sum_{j\notin A\cup\{i\}}X_{j}\Big)^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}(Y_{i}-Y_{i}^{\prime})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j\neq i}X_{j}\Big)^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}(Y_{i}-Y_{i}^{\prime})\cdot(X_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}\Big(\sum_{j\in A}Y_{j}^{\prime}+\sum_{j\notin A\cup\{i\}}Y_{j}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\Big[(X_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}\Big(\sum_{j\neq i}Y_{j}\Big)\cdot\Big(\sum_{j\in A}X_{j}^{\prime}+\sum_{j\notin A\cup\{i\}}X_{j}\Big)^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}(Y_{i}-Y_{i}^{\prime})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}^{\prime}\Big[(X_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}\Big(\sum_{j\neq i}Y_{j}\Big)\cdot(X_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}\Big(\sum_{j\in A}Y_{j}^{\prime}+\sum_{j\notin A\cup\{i\}}Y_{j}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\Big(\sum_{j\neq i}X_{j}\Big)^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}(Y_{i}Y_{i}^{\top}+\Sigma_{Y})\Sigma_{YX}\Big(\sum_{j\notin A\cup\{i\}}X_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\Big(\sum_{j\neq i}X_{j}\Big)^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}(Y_{i}X_{i}^{\top}+\Sigma_{YX})\Sigma_{XY}\Big(\sum_{j\notin A\cup\{i\}}Y_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\Big(\sum_{j\neq i}Y_{j}\Big)^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}(X_{i}Y_{i}^{\top}+\Sigma_{XY})\Sigma_{YX}\Big(\sum_{j\notin A\cup\{i\}}X_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\Big(\sum_{j\neq i}Y_{j}\Big)^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}(X_{i}X_{i}^{\top}+\Sigma_{X})\Sigma_{XY}\Big(\sum_{j\notin A\cup\{i\}}Y_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle\equiv S_{1}(i,A)+S_{2}(i,A)+S_{3}(i,A)+S_{4}(i,A).

Hence by using (
E.2
) we have

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{2}\sum_{A\subsetneq[n]}\sum_{i\notin A}\frac{S_{1}(i,A)}{{n\choose|A|}(n-|A|)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq n\\
j\neq i,k\neq i\end{subarray}}\big[X_{j}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}(Y_{i}Y_{i}^{\top}+\Sigma_{Y})\Sigma_{YX}X_{k}\big]\cdot\Big[\sum_{A\cap\{i,k\}=\emptyset}\frac{1}{{n\choose|A|}(n-|A|)}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq n\\
j\neq i,k\neq i\end{subarray}}\big[X_{j}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}(Y_{i}Y_{i}^{\top}+\Sigma_{Y})\Sigma_{YX}X_{k}\big].

Using similar calculations, we have

\displaystyle S_{2}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{2}\sum_{A\subsetneq[n]}\sum_{i\notin A}\frac{S_{2}(i,A)}{{n\choose|A|}(n-|A|)}=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq n\\
j\neq i,k\neq i\end{subarray}}\big[X_{j}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}(Y_{i}X_{i}^{\top}+\Sigma_{YX})\Sigma_{XY}Y_{k}\big],

\displaystyle S_{3}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{2}\sum_{A\subsetneq[n]}\sum_{i\notin A}\frac{S_{3}(i,A)}{{n\choose|A|}(n-|A|)}=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq n\\
j\neq i,k\neq i\end{subarray}}\big[Y_{j}^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}(X_{i}Y_{i}^{\top}+\Sigma_{XY})\Sigma_{YX}X_{k}\big],

\displaystyle S_{4}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{2}\sum_{A\subsetneq[n]}\sum_{i\notin A}\frac{S_{4}(i,A)}{{n\choose|A|}(n-|A|)}=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq n\\
j\neq i,k\neq i\end{subarray}}\big[Y_{j}^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}(X_{i}X_{i}^{\top}+\Sigma_{X})\Sigma_{XY}Y_{k}\big].

As
\mathbb{E}(\psi_{2}|\bm{X},\bm{Y})=\sum_{i=1}^{4}S_{i}
, the proof is complete by invoking Lemma
E.4
below.

(2). By definition, we have for each
i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\Delta_{i}\psi_{2}(\bm{X},\bm{Y})|^{3}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\Big|\Big(\sum_{j\neq i}X_{j})^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}(Y_{i}-Y_{i}^{\prime})\Big|^{3}+\mathbb{E}\Big|\Big(\sum_{j\neq i}Y_{j})^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}(X_{i}-X_{i}^{\prime})\Big|^{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(n^{2}\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{2})^{4}\Big)^{3/4}\leq n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Z_{1}^{\top}G_{[12]}Z_{2})^{6}\asymp n^{3/2}\lVert G_{[12]}\rVert_{F}^{3}\asymp n^{3/2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{3}.

The proof is complete.∎

Lemma E.4
.

Recall the terms
S_{1}
-
S_{4}
defined in the proof of Proposition
10.5
. The following variance bound holds.

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq 4}\var(S_{i})\lesssim_{M}n^{4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}.

Proof.

We only give a bound for
S_{1}
; bounds for
S_{2}
-
S_{4}
follow from completely analogous arguments. By definition we have
S_{1}=4^{-1}(S_{11}+S_{12})
, where

\displaystyle S_{11}

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq n\\
j\neq i,k\neq i\end{subarray}}(X_{j}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{i})(Y_{i}^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}X_{k})=\sum_{k=1}^{n}Z_{k}^{\top}\Big(\sum_{\ell\neq k}G_{[21]}Z_{\ell}\Big)\Big(\sum_{\ell\neq k}Z_{\ell}^{\top}G_{[12]}\Big)Z_{k},

\displaystyle S_{12}

\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq n\\
j\neq i,k\neq i\end{subarray}}X_{j}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}\Sigma_{YX}X_{k}=\sum_{j,k=1}^{n}\big[(n-2)+\delta_{jk}\big]X_{j}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}\Sigma_{YX}X_{k}.

To control the variance of
S_{11}
, note that for any
\ell\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{X_{\ell}}S_{11}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq n\\
j\neq i,k\neq i\end{subarray}}\delta_{j\ell}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{i}(Y_{i}^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}X_{k})+\delta_{k\ell}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{i}(Y_{i}^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}X_{j})

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i\neq\ell,j\neq i}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{i}(Y_{i}^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}X_{j})=2\sum_{j}\Sigma_{XY}M_{Y,-\{j,\ell\}}\Sigma_{YX}X_{j}.

Here
M_{Y,-A}\equiv\sum_{k\notin A}Y_{k}Y_{k}^{\top}
for
A\subset[n]
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert\nabla_{X_{\ell}}S_{11}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=4\sum_{j}\mathbb{E}\tr\big(\Sigma_{XY}M_{Y,-\{j,\ell\}}\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{XY}M_{Y,-\{j,\ell\}}\Sigma_{YX}\big)

\displaystyle=4\sum_{j}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\neq j,\ell}\mathbb{E}(Y_{k_{1}}^{\top}\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{k_{2}})^{2}\lesssim_{M}n^{3}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq n^{3}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}.

On the other hand, as

\displaystyle\nabla_{Y_{\ell}}S_{11}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq n\\
j\neq i,k\neq i\end{subarray}}\delta_{i\ell}\big[\Sigma_{YX}X_{j}(X_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{i})+\Sigma_{YX}X_{k}(X_{j}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{i})\big]=2\sum_{j,k\neq\ell}\Sigma_{YX}X_{j}(X_{k}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{\ell}),

we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert\nabla_{Y_{\ell}}S_{11}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=4\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2},k_{1},k_{2}\neq\ell,\\
\lvert\{j_{1},j_{2},k_{1},k_{2}\}\rvert=1,2\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\big(X_{j_{1}}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}\Sigma_{YX}X_{j_{2}}\big)\big(X_{k_{1}}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}X_{k_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}n\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}+n^{2}\big(\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}+\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\rVert_{F}^{2}\big)\asymp n^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}.

Putting together the two estimates and using the Poincaré inequality and a change of variable yield that

\displaystyle\var(S_{11})\lesssim_{M}\sum_{\ell}\big(\mathbb{E}\lVert\nabla_{X_{\ell}}S_{11}\rVert^{2}+\mathbb{E}\lVert\nabla_{Y_{\ell}}S_{11}\rVert^{2}\big)\lesssim_{M}n^{4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}.

The variance bound for
S_{12}
follows from a similar and simpler argument so we omit the proof. Putting together the estimates for
S_{11}
and
S_{12}
concludes the proof for
S_{1}
.
∎

E.4.
Proof of Proposition
10.6

We will bound the
X
component, and the bound for the
Y
component is completely analogous.

(1). By direct calculation, we have for each
i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}\psi_{3}(\bm{X},\bm{Y})=\tau_{X}^{-2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}(\lVert X_{i}\rVert^{2}-\lVert X_{i}^{\prime}\rVert^{2}).

Hence for any
A\subset[n]
such that
i\notin A
, we have also
\Delta_{i}\psi_{3}(\bm{X}^{A},\bm{Y}^{A})=\tau_{X}^{-2}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}(\lVert X_{i}\rVert^{2}-\lVert X_{i}^{\prime}\rVert^{2})
, thus by Lemma
E.2

\displaystyle T_{\psi_{3}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{A\subsetneq[n]}\sum_{i\notin A}\frac{\tau_{X}^{-4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}(\lVert X_{i}\rVert^{2}-\lVert X_{i}^{\prime}\rVert^{2})^{2}}{{n\choose|A|}(n-|A|)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{X}^{-4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}(\lVert X_{i}\rVert^{2}-\lVert X_{i}^{\prime}\rVert^{2})^{2}\cdot\Big[\sum_{A\subsetneq[n]:i\notin A}\frac{1}{{n\choose|A|}(n-|A|)}\Big]

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau_{X}^{-4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}(\lVert X_{i}\rVert^{2}-\lVert X_{i}^{\prime}\rVert^{2})^{2}.

This means

\displaystyle\var\big[\mathbb{E}(T_{\psi_{3}}|\bm{X},\bm{Y})\big]

\displaystyle\leq\var(T_{\psi_{3}})=n\tau_{X}^{-8}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{8}\cdot\var\Big[(\lVert X\rVert^{2}-\lVert X^{\prime}\rVert^{2})^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim n\tau_{X}^{-8}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{8}\cdot\mathbb{E}\big(\lVert X\rVert^{2}-\tr(\Sigma_{X})\big)^{4}\lesssim_{M}n\tau_{X}^{-4}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{8}.

(2). We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\Delta_{j}\psi_{3}(\bm{X},\bm{Y})|^{3}

\displaystyle=\tau_{X}^{-6}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{6}\cdot\mathbb{E}\lvert\lVert X\rVert^{2}-\lVert X^{\prime}\rVert^{2}\rvert^{3}\lesssim\tau_{X}^{-6}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{6}\mathbb{E}\lvert\lVert X\rVert^{2}-\tr(\Sigma_{X})\rvert^{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{X}^{-6}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{6}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}^{3}\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-3}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{6}.

The proof is complete.∎

Appendix F
Proof of Theorem
2.6

For notational simplicity, we only prove the case
f_{X}=f_{Y}=f
; the general case only requires formal modifications.

F.1.
Notation and preliminaries

Let

\displaystyle U_{f,\gamma_{X}}(x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle\equiv f\big(\lVert x_{1}-x_{2}\rVert/\gamma_{X}\big)-\mathbb{E}f\big(\lVert x_{1}-X\rVert/\gamma_{X}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}f\big(\lVert X-x_{2}\rVert/\gamma_{X}\big)+\mathbb{E}f\big(\lVert X-X^{\prime}\rVert/\gamma_{X}\big),

\displaystyle V_{f,\gamma_{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})

\displaystyle\equiv f\big(\lVert y_{1}-y_{2}\rVert/\gamma_{Y}\big)-\mathbb{E}f\big(\lVert y_{1}-Y\rVert/\gamma_{Y}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\mathbb{E}f\big(\lVert Y-y_{2}\rVert/\gamma_{Y}\big)+\mathbb{E}f\big(\lVert Y-Y^{\prime}\rVert/\gamma_{Y}\big).

The sample kernel distance covariance
\dcov_{\ast}^{2}(\bm{X},\bm{Y};f,\gamma)
in (
2.12
) can be represented as a 4-th order
U
-statistics using
U_{f,\gamma_{X}},V_{f,\gamma_{Y}}
similar to Proposition
2.1
.

Proposition F.1
.

The following holds:

\displaystyle\dcov_{\ast}^{2}(\bm{X},\bm{Y};f,\gamma)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\binom{n}{4}}\sum_{i_{1}<\cdots<i_{4}}h_{f,\gamma}\big(Z_{i_{1}},Z_{i_{2}},Z_{i_{3}},Z_{i_{4}}\big),

where

\displaystyle h_{f,\gamma}(Z_{1},Z_{2},Z_{3},Z_{4})=\frac{1}{4!}\sum_{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{4})\in\sigma(1,2,3,4)}\bigg[U_{f,\gamma_{X}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V_{f,\gamma_{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{2}})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+U_{f,\gamma_{X}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V_{f,\gamma_{Y}}(Y_{i_{3}},Y_{i_{4}})-2U_{f,\gamma_{X}}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})V_{f,\gamma_{Y}}(Y_{i_{1}},Y_{i_{3}})\bigg].

Proof.

The proof follows the same lines as that of Proposition
2.1
; we omit repetitive details.
∎

We need the following basic properties of
f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}(\cdot)
inherited from Assumption
B
.

Lemma F.2
.

Under Assumption
B
for
f(\cdot)
, conclusions (1)-(4) therein hold for
f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}(\cdot)
with different constants
c^{\prime}_{\varepsilon},C^{\prime}_{\varepsilon},\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}
. Furthermore,
c^{\prime}_{\varepsilon},C^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
only depend on
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}
and
\varepsilon
, and
\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}
only depends on
\mathfrak{q}
.

Proof.

The claims hold by definitions of
f^{(\ell)}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}
,
\ell=0,1,2,3,4
.
∎

A useful consequence of Assumption
B
is the following.

Lemma F.3
.

Suppose that Assumption
B
holds, and the spectrum of
\Sigma_{X},\Sigma_{Y}
is contained in
[1/M,M]
for some
M>1
. Then for any integer
s\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
K\equiv K(s,\mathfrak{q})>0
such that if
p\wedge q\geq K
,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq\ell\leq 4}\sup_{\rho^{2}\in[1/M,M]}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\mathbb{E}\big\lvert f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{(\ell)}\big(\rho^{2}(1+tW)\big)\big\rvert^{s}\lesssim_{M}1,

where
W
is either
L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})
or
L_{Y}(Y_{1},Y_{2})
defined in (
7.3
).

Proof.

We only prove the case for
W=L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})
,
\ell=1
. By Lemma
F.2
, for all
x\geq 0
,
\lvert f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{(1)}(x)\rvert\leq C_{f}(1\vee x^{-\mathfrak{q^{\prime}}})
for some
C_{f}>0
and
\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
. This means that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\lvert f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{(1)}\big(\rho^{2}(1+tL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))\big)\big\rvert^{s}\lesssim 1+\rho^{-2s\mathfrak{q^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}\big(1+tL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2})\big)^{-s\mathfrak{q^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\rho^{-2s\mathfrak{q^{\prime}}}\min\big\{(1-t)^{-s\mathfrak{q^{\prime}}},t^{-s\mathfrak{q^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}\big(\tau_{X}^{-2}\lVert X_{1}-X_{2}\rVert^{2}\big)^{-s\mathfrak{q^{\prime}}}\}.

Now use (
B.4
) to conclude.
∎

We define the analogue of the
h
function in (
7.1
): For
u\geq-1
, let

\displaystyle h_{f,\rho}(u)

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}(\rho^{2}(1+u))-f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}(\rho^{2})-f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho^{2})\rho^{2}u}{2f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho^{2})\rho^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho^{2}}{2f^{\prime}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}(\rho^{2})}\cdot u^{2}\int_{0}^{1}f^{\prime\prime}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}\big(\rho^{2}(1+su)\big)(1-s)\,\mathrm{d}s.

When
f(x)=x
so that
f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}(x)=\sqrt{x}
and
\rho=1
, we recover the
h
function in (
7.1
).

Lemma F.4
.

Suppose that Assumption
B
holds and
1/M\leq\rho^{2}\leq M
for some
M>1
. Then

\displaystyle\lvert h_{f,\rho}(u)\rvert\lesssim_{M}u^{2},\quad\lvert h_{f,\rho}^{\prime}(u)\rvert\lesssim_{M}\big|u\cdot F_{f,\rho;2}(u)\big|,

where

\displaystyle F_{f,\rho;2}(u)\equiv u^{-1}\int_{0}^{u}f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{2}(1+t))\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{1}f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{2}(1+us))\,\mathrm{d}s.

Furthermore

\displaystyle h_{f,\rho}(u)=\frac{f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{2})\rho^{2}}{4f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho^{2})}\cdot u^{2}+\frac{\rho^{4}}{2f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho^{2})}\cdot u^{3}\int_{0}^{1}f^{(3)}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}\big(\rho^{2}(1+su)\big)\frac{(1-s)^{2}}{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{2})\rho^{2}}{4f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho^{2})}\cdot u^{2}+\frac{f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{(3)}(\rho^{2})\rho^{4}}{12f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho^{2})}\cdot u^{3}+\frac{\rho^{6}}{2f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho^{2})}\cdot u^{4}\int_{0}^{1}f^{(4)}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}\big(\rho^{2}(1+su)\big)\frac{(1-s)^{3}}{6}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{2})\rho^{2}}{4f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho^{2})}\cdot u^{2}+h_{f,\rho;3}(u).

Proof.

The bound for
h_{f,\rho}
follows by the following consideration: if
u\in[-1,-1/2]
, it holds by Lemma
F.2
-Conclusion (2) and the fact
\rho\asymp_{M}1
that
|h_{f,\rho}(u)|\lesssim_{M}1\lesssim_{M}u^{2}
; if
u>-1/2
, if follows from the lower bound on
|f^{\prime}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}|
and upper bound on
|f^{{}^{\prime\prime}}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}|
that

\displaystyle|h_{f,\rho}(u)|

\displaystyle=\Big|\frac{\rho^{2}}{2f^{\prime}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}(\rho^{2})}\cdot u^{2}\int_{0}^{1}f^{\prime\prime}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}\big(\rho^{2}(1+su)\big)(1-s)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}u^{2}\cdot\sup_{u>-1/2}\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\big|f^{\prime\prime}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}\big(\rho^{2}(1+su)\big)\big|\lesssim_{M}u^{2}.

On the other hand, by Taylor expansion

\displaystyle f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}(\rho^{2}(1+u))-f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}(\rho^{2})-f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho^{2})\rho^{2}u=\int_{0}^{\rho^{2}u}f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{2}+t)(\rho^{2}u-t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{2})}{2}(\rho^{2}u)^{2}+(\rho^{2}u)^{3}\int_{0}^{1}f^{(3)}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}\big(\rho^{2}(1+su)\big)\frac{(1-s)^{2}}{2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{2})}{2}(\rho^{2}u)^{2}+\frac{f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{(3)}(\rho^{2})}{6}(\rho^{2}u)^{3}+(\rho^{2}u)^{4}\int_{0}^{1}f^{(4)}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}\big(\rho^{2}(1+su)\big)\frac{(1-s)^{3}}{6}\,\mathrm{d}s.

The first equality shows that

\displaystyle\lvert h_{f,\rho}^{\prime}(u)\rvert=\bigg\lvert\frac{1}{2f^{\prime}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}(\rho^{2})}\int_{0}^{\rho^{2}u}f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{2}+t)\,\mathrm{d}t\bigg\rvert\lesssim_{M}\bigg\lvert\int_{0}^{u}f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime\prime}(\rho^{2}(1+t))\,\mathrm{d}t\bigg\rvert=\big|u\cdot F_{f,\rho;2}(u)\big|,

as desired.
∎

F.2.
Residual estimates

Let

\displaystyle R_{X;f}(x_{1},x_{2})\equiv h_{f,\rho_{X}}(L_{X}(x_{1},x_{2})),\,R_{Y;f}(y_{1},y_{2})\equiv h_{f,\rho_{Y}}(L_{Y}(y_{1},y_{2})).

These quantities are analogues of
R_{X},R_{Y}
defined in (
7.3
). Now we may develop an expansion of
U_{f,\gamma_{X}},V_{f,\gamma_{Y}}
similar to Lemma
7.4
.

Lemma F.5
.

With
\bar{R}_{X;f},\bar{R}_{Y;f}
denoting the double centered versions of
{R}_{X;f},{R}_{Y;f}
, we have

\displaystyle U_{f,\gamma_{X}}(x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle=-\frac{2f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho_{X}^{2})}{\gamma_{X}^{2}}\Big[x_{1}^{\top}x_{2}-\tau_{X}^{2}\bar{R}_{X;f}(L_{X}(x_{1},x_{2}))\Big],

\displaystyle V_{f,\gamma_{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})

\displaystyle=-\frac{2f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho_{Y}^{2})}{\gamma_{Y}^{2}}\Big[y_{1}^{\top}y_{2}-\tau_{Y}^{2}\bar{R}_{Y;f}(L_{Y}(y_{1},y_{2}))\Big].

Proof.

Using the definition of
R_{X,f}
, we have

\displaystyle f\bigg(\frac{\lVert x_{1}-x_{2}\rVert}{\gamma_{X}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}\big(\rho_{X}^{2}+\rho_{X}^{2}L_{X}(x_{1},x_{2})\big)

\displaystyle=f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}(\rho_{X}^{2})+f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho_{X}^{2})\rho_{X}^{2}\Big[L_{X}(x_{1},x_{2})+2\cdot R_{X;f}(L_{X}(x_{1},x_{2}))\Big],

The claim follows by using the fact that double centered version of
L_{X}(x_{1},x_{2})
equals
-2x_{1}^{\top}x_{2}/\tau_{X}^{2}
.
∎

The following moment estimates give an analogy of Lemma
7.5
.

Lemma F.6
.

Suppose Assumption
B
holds, and that (i) the spectrum of
\Sigma_{X},\Sigma_{Y}
and (ii)
\rho_{X},\rho_{Y}
are contained in
[1/M,M]
for some
M>1
. Then:

(1)

For any positive integer
s\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\tau_{X}^{2s}\mathbb{E}R_{X;f}^{s}(X_{1},X_{2})\bigvee\tau_{Y}^{2s}\mathbb{E}R_{Y;f}^{s}(Y_{1},Y_{2})\lesssim_{M,s}1.

(2)

For any positive integer
s\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\tau_{X}^{s}\mathbb{E}h_{f,\rho_{X}}^{\prime}(L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))^{s}\bigvee\tau_{Y}^{s}\mathbb{E}h_{f,\rho_{Y}}^{\prime}(L_{Y}(Y_{1},Y_{2}))^{s}\lesssim_{M}1

holds for
p,q
large (possibly depending on
s,\mathfrak{q}
).

Proof.

Combining with estimates in Lemma
7.5
, (1) follows by
\lvert h_{f,\rho}(u)\rvert\lesssim_{M}u^{2}
, and (2) follows by
\lvert h^{\prime}_{f,\rho}(u)\rvert\lesssim_{M}|u\cdot F_{f,\rho;2}(u)|
and noting that for any
s\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lvert F_{f,\rho;2}(L_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))\rvert^{s}\lesssim\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\mathbb{E}\big\lvert f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{(2)}\big(\rho^{2}(1+tL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))\big)\big\rvert^{s}\lesssim_{M}1

by Lemma
F.3
.
∎

Let

\displaystyle\psi_{X;f}(x_{1},y_{1})

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbb{E}_{X_{2},Y_{2}}[\bar{R}_{X;f}(x_{1},X_{2})Y_{2}^{\top}y_{1}],

\displaystyle\psi_{Y;f}(x_{1},y_{1})

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbb{E}_{X_{2},Y_{2}}[\bar{R}_{Y;f}(y_{1},Y_{2})X_{2}^{\top}x_{1}],

\displaystyle\psi_{X,Y;f}(x_{1},y_{1})

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbb{E}_{X_{2},Y_{2}}[\bar{R}_{X;f}(x_{1},X_{2})\bar{R}_{Y;f}(y_{1},Y_{2})].

Proposition F.7
.

Suppose that Assumption
B
holds, and that
\rho_{X},\rho_{Y}
are contained in
[1/M,M]
for some
M>1
. Then the bounds in Proposition
8.2
hold when
\psi_{X},\psi_{Y},\psi_{X,Y}
are replaced with
\psi_{X;f},\psi_{Y;f},\psi_{X,Y;f}
under the same spectrum conditions, for
p,q
large (possibly depending on
\mathfrak{q}
).

Proof.

The proof essentially proceeds the same way as in the proof of Proposition
8.2
. We sketch some differences.

(
First moment
) First, using a similar argument as in Lemma
8.1
, we have the decomposition

\displaystyle\psi_{X;f}(x,y)=A_{1,X;f}(x,y)+A_{2,X;f}(x,y),

where
A_{2,X;f}(x,y)=\mathbb{E}\big[h_{f,\rho_{X};3}(L_{X}(x,X))Y^{\top}y\big]
(
h_{f,\rho_{X};3}
defined in Lemma
F.4
), and

\displaystyle A_{1,X;f}(x,y)=-\frac{f^{\prime\prime}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}(\rho_{X}^{2})\rho_{X}^{2}}{f^{\prime}_{\sqrt{\cdot}}(\rho_{X}^{2})\tau_{X}^{4}}\Big[\big(\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\tr(\Sigma_{X})\big)x^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}y+2x^{\top}\Sigma_{X}\Sigma_{XY}y\Big].

The bound
|\mathbb{E}A_{1,X;f}(X,Y)|\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
follows from Lemma
F.2
, and the bound for
|\mathbb{E}A_{2,X;f}(X_{1},Y_{1})|
follows from the following modification: by Lemma
F.2
,

\displaystyle\lvert\mathbb{E}A_{2,X;f}(X_{1},Y_{1})\rvert\lesssim_{M}\big\lvert\mathbb{E}\big[L_{X}^{3}(X_{1},X_{2})Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2}\big]\big\rvert

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigg[L_{X}^{4}(X_{1},X_{2})Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2}\cdot\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)^{3}f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{(4)}\big(\rho^{2}(1+sL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg]\bigg\rvert.

The first term can be handled by Lemma
C.3
of order at most
\tau_{X}^{-4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}
, while by Lemma
F.3
and a similar argument as in Lemma
C.3
, the second term can be bounded, up to a constant depending on
M
, by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{1/2}L_{X}^{8}(X_{1},X_{2})\bigg[\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)^{3}f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{(4)}\big(\rho^{2}(1+sL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg]^{2}\cdot\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\big[f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{(4)}\big(\rho^{2}(1+sL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))\big)\big]^{4}\lesssim_{M,\mathfrak{q}}\tau_{X}^{-4}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

(
Second moment
) The proof of the second moment bounds for
\psi_{X;f},\psi_{Y;f}
requires a modification for controlling
\mathbb{E}A_{2,X;f}^{2}(X_{1},Y_{1})
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}A_{2,X;f}^{2}(X_{1},Y_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\mathbb{E}_{X_{1},Y_{1}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{X_{2},Y_{2}}\bigg[L_{X}^{3}(X_{1},X_{2})Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)^{2}f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{(3)}\big(\rho^{2}(1+sL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg]\bigg\}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-6}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\cdot\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\mathbb{E}^{1/4}\big[f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{(3)}\big(\rho^{2}(1+sL_{X}(X_{1},X_{2}))\big)\big]^{8}\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{-6}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}.

using Lemma
F.3
in the last step. The proof of the second moment bound for
\psi_{X,Y;f}
proceeds along the same lines upon using Lemma
F.6
.
∎

F.3.
Mean and variance expansion

Now we may prove the mean and variance expansion formulae:

Proposition F.8
.

Suppose that Assumption
B
holds, and that (i) the spectrum of
\Sigma_{X},\Sigma_{Y}
(ii)
\rho_{X},\rho_{Y}
are contained in
[1/M,M]
for some
M>1
.

(1)

The following mean expansion holds:

\displaystyle m_{\Sigma;f,\gamma}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathbb{E}\dcov_{\ast}^{2}(\bm{X},\bm{Y};f,\gamma)=\dcov^{2}(X,Y;f,\gamma)

\displaystyle=\varrho(\gamma)m_{\Sigma}\Big[1+\mathcal{O}_{M}\big((\tau_{X}\wedge\tau_{Y})^{-1}\big)\Big],

where
m_{\Sigma}
is the mean for distance covariance defined in Theorem
8.4
.

(2)

The following variance expansion holds:

\displaystyle\var\big(\dcov_{\ast}^{2}(\bm{X},\bm{Y};f,\gamma)\big)

\displaystyle=\varrho^{2}(\gamma)\bar{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(X,Y)\Big[1+\mathcal{O}_{M}\big(n^{-1/2}+(p\wedge q)^{-1/4}\big)\Big].

Here
\bar{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(X,Y)
is defined in Theorem
2.2
.

Proof.

The mean and variance expansions can be proved using exactly the same lines as in the proof of Theorems
8.4
and
9.12
, but using Proposition
F.7
. The scaling can be checked, e.g., as the leading term in
m_{\Sigma;f,\gamma}
equals, by Lemma
F.5
,

\displaystyle\frac{4f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho_{X}^{2})f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho_{Y}^{2})}{\gamma_{X}^{2}\tau_{Y}^{2}}\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}=4f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho_{X}^{2})f_{\sqrt{\cdot}}^{\prime}(\rho_{Y}^{2})\frac{\tau_{X}\tau_{Y}}{\gamma_{X}^{2}\gamma_{Y}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{\tau_{X}\tau_{Y}}=\varrho(\gamma)\cdot\frac{\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}}{\tau_{X}\tau_{Y}},

whereas
\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}/(\tau_{X}\tau_{Y})
is the leading term in
m_{\Sigma}
as established in Theorem
8.4
.
∎

The rest of the proof of Theorem
2.6
follows exactly the same lines as that of the proof of Theorem
2.2
. Details are omitted.

Appendix G
Auxiliary lemmas

Lemma G.1
.

For real-valued random variables
W,R
,
N\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and a deterministic real number
D
, we have

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{Kol}}(W+R+D,N)\leq d_{\mathrm{Kol}}(W,N)+2(\mathbb{E}R^{2})^{1/3}+\lvert D\rvert.

Proof.

Fix
u>0
, on an event
E_{u}
with probability at least
1-u^{-2}
,
\lvert R\rvert\leq u\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}R^{2}
. Then for any
t\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(W+R+D\leq t)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(W+D\leq t+u\mathbb{E}^{1/2}R^{2})+u^{-2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(N\leq t+\lvert D\rvert+u\mathbb{E}^{1/2}R^{2})+u^{-2}+d_{\mathrm{Kol}}(W,N)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(N\leq t)+\lvert D\rvert+u\mathbb{E}^{1/2}R^{2}+u^{-2}+d_{\mathrm{Kol}}(W,N).

Optimizing the above display over
u>0
to conclude one side of the inequality. The reverse direction is similar.
∎

Lemma G.2
.

For two random variables
X,Y
, with
\varphi_{Y}
denoting the Lebesgue density of
Y
, we have
d_{\mathrm{Kol}}^{2}(X,Y)\leq 4\lVert\varphi_{Y}\rVert_{\infty}d_{\mathrm{W}}(X,Y)
, where
d_{\mathrm{W}}
is the Wasserstein distance.

Proof.

Fix
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
\varepsilon>0
. Let
g_{t,\varepsilon}(\cdot)\equiv 1
on
(-\infty,t]
,
g_{t,\varepsilon}(\cdot)\equiv 0
on
[t+\varepsilon,\infty)
and be linearly interpolated on
(t,t+\varepsilon)
. As
g_{t,\varepsilon}
is
\varepsilon^{-1}
-Lipschitz,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X\leq t)-\mathbb{P}(Y\leq t)\leq\mathbb{E}g_{t,\varepsilon}(X)-\mathbb{E}g_{t,\varepsilon}(Y)+\mathbb{E}g_{t,\varepsilon}(Y)-\mathbb{E}\bm{1}_{(-\infty,t]}(Y)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-1}d_{\mathrm{W}}(X,Y)+\mathbb{P}\big(t\leq Y\leq t+\varepsilon\big)\leq\varepsilon^{-1}d_{\mathrm{W}}(X,Y)+\varepsilon\lVert\varphi_{Y}\rVert_{\infty}.

Optimizing over
\varepsilon>0
obtains the one-sided inequality. The other direction is similar.
∎

Lemma G.3
.

Suppose
Z,Z_{1},Z_{2},\ldots
are independent copies of a random vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
having i.i.d. symmetric components with mean
0
, variance
1
, and fourth moment
m_{4}
. Let
\kappa\equiv m_{4}-3
be the excess kurtosis. The following hold.

(1)

For any matrices
A,B\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z^{\top}AZ)(Z^{\top}BZ)=\tr(A)\tr(B)+\tr(AB)+\tr(AB^{\top})+\kappa\tr(A\circ B),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big(Z^{\top}AZ-\tr(A)\big)\big(Z^{\top}BZ-\tr(B)\big)=\tr(AB)+\tr(AB^{\top})+\kappa\tr(A\circ B).

(2)

For any matrices
A,B\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}^{\top}AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}BZ_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle=2\big(\tr^{2}(AB)+\tr(ABAB)+\tr(ABB^{\top}A^{\top})+\tr(A^{\top}B^{\top}BA)\big)+\lVert A\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert B\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle+2\kappa\cdot\big[\tr(BA\circ BA)+\tr(AB\circ AB)+\tr(AA^{\top}\circ BB^{\top}\big]+\kappa^{2}\cdot\tr\big((A\circ A)(B\circ B)\big).

(3)

For any matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(ZZ^{\top})(Z^{\top}AZ)=A+A^{\top}+\tr(A)I_{d}+\kappa\cdot I_{d}\circ A.

Proof.

(1). Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z^{\top}AZ)(Z^{\top}BZ)=\mathbb{E}\tr\big(AZZ^{\top}BZZ^{\top}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\sum_{i,j,k,\ell}A_{ij}(ZZ^{\top})_{jk}B_{k\ell}(ZZ^{\top})_{\ell i}=\sum_{i,j,k,\ell}A_{ij}B_{k\ell}\cdot\mathbb{E}Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{k}Z_{\ell}.

To make the expectation on the right hand side non-vanishing:

•

(i=j)\neq(k=\ell)
. This contributes to
\sum_{i\neq k}A_{ii}B_{kk}
.

•

(i=k)\neq(j=\ell)
. This contributes to
\sum_{i,j}A_{ij}B_{ij}
.

•

(i=\ell)\neq(j=k)
. This contributes to
\sum_{i,j}A_{ij}B_{ji}
.

•

i=j=k=\ell
. This contributes to
m_{4}\sum_{i}A_{ii}B_{ii}=m_{4}\tr(A\circ B)
.

Collecting the terms to conclude the first equality. The second equality follows a direct expansion.

(2). Using part (1), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}^{\top}AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}BZ_{1})^{2}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{1}^{\top}\big(AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}B\big)Z_{1}\cdot Z_{1}^{\top}\big(AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}B\big)Z_{1}\lvert Z_{2}\big]\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\tr^{2}(AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}B)+\mathbb{E}\tr(AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}BAZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}B)+\mathbb{E}\tr(AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}BB^{\top}Z_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}A^{\top})

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa\mathbb{E}\tr(AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}B\circ AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}B).

Using again part (1), the above terms can be calculated as follows:

•

The firs term is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\tr^{2}(AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}B)=\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}BAZ_{2})^{2}=\tr^{2}(BA)+\tr(BABA)+\tr(BAA^{\top}B^{\top})+\kappa\tr(BA\circ BA).

•

Since
\mathbb{E}\tr(AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}BAZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}B)=\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}BAZ_{2})
, the second term equals the first term.

•

The third term is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\tr(AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}BB^{\top}Z_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}A^{\top})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}BB^{\top}Z_{2})(Z_{2}^{\top}A^{\top}AZ_{2})

\displaystyle=\lVert A\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert B\rVert_{F}^{2}+2\tr(BB^{\top}A^{\top}A)+\kappa\cdot\tr(BB^{\top}\circ AA^{\top}).

•

The fourth term satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\tr(AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}B\circ AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}B)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}(AZ_{2}Z_{2}^{\top}B)_{ii}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}(Z_{2}^{\top}Be_{i}e_{i}^{\top}AZ_{2})^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Big[\tr^{2}(Be_{i}e_{i}^{\top}A)+\tr(Be_{i}e_{i}^{\top}ABe_{i}e_{i}^{\top}A)+\tr(Be_{i}e_{i}^{\top}AA^{\top}e_{i}e_{i}^{\top}B^{\top})+\kappa\cdot\tr(Be_{i}e_{i}^{\top}A\circ Be_{i}e_{i}^{\top}A)\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Big[(AB)_{ii}^{2}+(AB)_{ii}^{2}+(AA^{\top})_{ii}(B^{\top}B)_{ii}+\kappa\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{d}A_{ij}^{2}B_{ji}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=2\tr(AB\circ AB)+\tr(AA^{\top}\circ BB^{\top})+\kappa\cdot\tr\big((A\circ A)(B\circ B)\big).

(3). For
i\neq j
,

\displaystyle\big(\mathbb{E}(ZZ^{\top})(Z^{\top}AZ)\big)_{ij}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[Z_{i}Z_{j}\sum_{k,\ell}Z_{k}A_{k\ell}Z_{\ell}\bigg]=A_{ij}+A_{ji}.

For
i=j
,

\displaystyle\big(\mathbb{E}(ZZ^{\top})(Z^{\top}AZ)\big)_{ii}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[Z_{i}^{2}\sum_{k,\ell}Z_{k}A_{k\ell}Z_{\ell}\bigg]=\mathbb{E}Z_{i}^{4}\cdot A_{ii}+\sum_{k\neq i}(\mathbb{E}Z_{i}^{2})(\mathbb{E}Z_{k}^{2})A_{kk}

\displaystyle=m_{4}A_{ii}+\sum_{k\neq i}A_{kk}=(m_{4}-1)A_{ii}+\tr(A).

The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma G.4
.

The following hold:

(1)

\mathbb{E}\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}\lVert Y_{1}\rVert^{2}=\tr(\Sigma_{X})\tr(\Sigma_{Y})+2\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\kappa\tr(H_{[11]}\circ H_{[22]})
.

(2)

\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{2})^{2}=\tr(\Sigma_{X}\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}\Sigma_{YX})
.

(3)

For any matrix
A,B
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{\top}AY_{1})(X_{1}^{\top}BY_{1})=\tr(A\Sigma_{YX})\tr(B\Sigma_{YX})+\tr(A\Sigma_{YX}B\Sigma_{YX})+\tr(\Sigma_{X}A\Sigma_{Y}B^{\top})+\kappa\cdot\tr(A_{\Sigma}\circ B_{\Sigma}).

Here
A_{\Sigma},B_{\Sigma}
are given in (
G.1
) below. In particular, for
A=B=\Sigma_{XY}
, we have
\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{1})^{2}=\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{4}+\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{YX}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\tr(\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X})+\kappa\cdot\tr(G_{[12]}\circ G_{[12]})
.

(4)

For any matrix
A,B
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{\top}AX_{1})(Y_{1}^{\top}BY_{1})=\tr(A\Sigma_{X})\tr(B\Sigma_{Y})+\tr(A\Sigma_{XY}B\Sigma_{YX})+\tr(A\Sigma_{XY}B^{\top}\Sigma_{YX})+\kappa\cdot\tr(\bar{A}_{\Sigma}\circ\bar{B}_{\Sigma}).

Here
\bar{A}_{\Sigma},\bar{B}_{\Sigma}
are given in (
G.2
) below.

Proof.

(1) follows from

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert X_{1}\rVert^{2}\lVert Y_{1}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}Z_{1}^{\top}H_{[11]}Z_{1}\cdot Z_{1}^{\top}H_{[22]}Z_{1}

\displaystyle=\tr(H_{[11]})\tr(H_{[22]})+2\tr(H_{[11]}H_{[22]})+\kappa\tr(H_{[11]}\circ H_{[22]})

\displaystyle=\tr(\Sigma_{X})\tr(\Sigma_{Y})+2\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}+\kappa\tr(H_{[11]}\circ H_{[22]}).

(2) follows from

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}Y_{2})^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\tr\big(\Sigma_{XY}Y_{2}Y_{2}^{\top}\Sigma_{XY}X_{1}X_{1}^{\top}\big)=\tr(\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}\Sigma_{YX}\Sigma_{X}).

(4). Let

(G.1)

\displaystyle A_{\Sigma}\equiv\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}0&A\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2},\quad B_{\Sigma}\equiv\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}0&B\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2}.

Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{\top}AY_{1})(X_{1}^{\top}BY_{1})=\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}^{\top}A_{\Sigma}Z_{1})(Z_{1}^{\top}B_{\Sigma}Z_{1})

\displaystyle=\tr(A_{\Sigma})\tr(B_{\Sigma})+\tr(A_{\Sigma}B_{\Sigma})+\tr(A_{\Sigma}B_{\Sigma}^{\top})+\kappa\cdot\tr(A_{\Sigma}\circ B_{\Sigma})

\displaystyle=\tr(A\Sigma_{YX})\tr(B\Sigma_{YX})+\tr(A\Sigma_{YX}B\Sigma_{YX})+\tr(\Sigma_{X}A\Sigma_{Y}B^{\top})+\kappa\cdot\tr(A_{\Sigma}\circ B_{\Sigma}).

(5). Let

(G.2)

\displaystyle\bar{A}_{\Sigma}\equiv\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}A&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2},\quad\bar{B}_{\Sigma}\equiv\Sigma^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&B\end{pmatrix}\Sigma^{1/2}.

Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{\top}AX_{1})(Y_{1}^{\top}BY_{1})=\mathbb{E}(Z_{1}^{\top}\bar{A}_{\Sigma}Z_{1})(Z_{1}^{\top}\bar{B}_{\Sigma}Z_{1})

\displaystyle=\tr(\bar{A}_{\Sigma})\tr(\bar{B}_{\Sigma})+\tr(\bar{A}_{\Sigma}\bar{B}_{\Sigma})+\tr(\bar{A}_{\Sigma}\bar{B}_{\Sigma}^{\top})+\kappa\tr(\bar{A}_{\Sigma}\circ\bar{B}_{\Sigma})

\displaystyle=\tr(A\Sigma_{X})\tr(B\Sigma_{Y})+\tr(A\Sigma_{XY}B\Sigma_{YX})+\tr(A\Sigma_{XY}B^{\top}\Sigma_{YX})+\kappa\tr(\bar{A}_{\Sigma}\circ\bar{B}_{\Sigma}).

The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma G.5
.

It holds that
\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F}
. If in addition
\lVert\Sigma_{X}^{-1}\rVert_{\op}\vee\lVert\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\rVert_{\op}\leq M
for some
M>1
, then
\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim_{M}\tau_{X}^{2}\wedge\tau_{Y}^{2}
.

Proof.

For the first claim, note that

\displaystyle\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\tr\big(\mathbb{E}X_{1}Y_{1}^{\top}\mathbb{E}Y_{2}X_{2}^{\top}\big)=\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})(Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2})\leq\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(X_{1}^{\top}X_{2})^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}^{1/2}(Y_{1}^{\top}Y_{2})^{2}=\lVert\Sigma_{X}\rVert_{F}\lVert\Sigma_{Y}\rVert_{F},

as desired. For the second claim, as
\Sigma_{X\backslash Y}=\Sigma_{X}-\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\Sigma_{YX}
is p.s.d. (since it is the conditional variance of
X
given
Y
when
(X^{\top},Y^{\top})^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma)
), we have

\displaystyle\lVert\Sigma_{XY}\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim_{M}\tr(\Sigma_{XY}\Sigma_{Y}^{-1}\Sigma_{YX})\leq\tr(\Sigma_{X})=\tau_{X}^{2}/2.

Flipping the role of
X,Y
to conclude.
∎
